- **Per-module schemas, first group (PR-DB-2.7).** Ten modules in one revision (`0024`) and one PR instead of ten: the pilot fixed the pattern, dropping nothing and moving no data, and each CI cycle costs about twenty minutes, so ten PRs would have bought ten sequential cycles for the same assurance (the compose replay and the Postgres role test cover every module in one run, and a failure names the module). The group is the modules that touch only their own tables and the shared ones, found by scanning each module's code for the shared libraries it uses (outbox, idempotency, module identity, periodic runs, audit) and for raw SQL (there is none outside the shared library). The reference rApps count as modules: they are separate services. **MLLF and the mocks have no database** (MLLF's chart entry says `database: false`), so there is no role to make. The rest (SME, DME, NFO, RApp Management, A1 Related, FOCOM, AIMgF, RAN NF OAM, R1 Termination) are the second group because they carry what needs a decision: R1 Termination reads RAN NF OAM's `rapp_kill` for the gateway's kill switch (an explicit, read-only grant to document, `read` in the manifest, and the gateway should name the schema instead of relying on the compatibility view), the outbox sweep and the worker's periodic runs sit in RAN NF OAM, and SME's tables are read on every request. **A check that was not checking:** the workflow's `! command` lines (this branch's and two older ones) asserted that something is absent, but a failed `!` does not stop a bash script run with `-e`, so they could not fail; each is now an `if`, and every one of them holds when run.
- **Per-module schemas, second group (PR-DB-2.7).** The nine modules that carried decisions. (1) *R1 Termination gets a role and no schema.* It owns no table; it writes the shared audit chain (`audit_log`, `audit_head`) and reads one table of RAN NF OAM's for the kill switch. That read is a `read` entry in the manifest (SELECT only, `ran_nf_oam.rapp_kill`), so a reviewer sees it and a test proves the role cannot change it; the gateway names the schema through `R1_KILL_SWITCH_SCHEMA` rather than relying on the compatibility view, which goes away one release later. Not done: moving the kill switch behind RAN NF OAM's API (the gateway would then call a module per change; the shared read is the cheaper design and is now explicit). (2) *RAN NF OAM and its worker share one role*: it is one SMO module (one identity, ST-4) that holds the outbox sweep and the periodic runs, so it is granted `periodic_run` and the outbox, and nobody else is. (3) *Shared tables per module are the ones its code uses* (outbox, idempotency keys, module identity), and a module that uses none of one is refused it; the compose replay would show a missing grant as a failed call, and does not. (4) *The CI checks ask the question per module, not for a fixed table*: `scripts/db_roles_denied.py` lists what a role must be refused (one table of each other module and of the shared tables, minus what it owns, is given or may read), because a fixed table is a table some module owns.
- **Postgres HA lab (PR-DB-7.2 to 7.4, PR-HA-3.2).** The ADR chose an operator; this is the proof. Decisions: (1) *CloudNativePG, operator and operand pinned* (manifest sha256, operand by digest) so the lane is reproducible; the operand is PostgreSQL 17.4 because 1.25 does not support 18, which the bundled lab Postgres uses, so the lane also shows the migrations are not tied to 18. (2) *The owner gets CREATEROLE through `postInitApplicationSQL`*, since the migrate Job makes the per-module roles; a managed service must grant the same or the roles be made by hand (already in the chart's notes). (3) *The chart installs with the replicas first in the host list and `target_session_attrs=read-write`*, the form that needs the driver to skip a standby, rather than the `-rw` service that would hide a mistake there; SQLAlchemy cannot parse a comma in the host part, so a list goes in the query (`?host=a,b&port=5432`), which it passes to the driver unchanged. (4) *The failure is an unplanned kill by force-deleting the pod*, the harder case; the measure is a write probe through SME (a row written and deleted every 250 ms) rather than `/health`, because a service can answer health while its database is gone. The longest gap is the recovery time the lane records. (5) *Not done, said so in OPEN_ITEMS*: a planned switchover (needs the cnpg plugin or a status patch) and the full runbook replay in the cluster, which needs a callback receiver and package server reachable from the pods; the e2e checks and the roles script run before and after instead. The job was written without a local cluster (no Docker in the sandbox), so its first CI run is also its first run.


- **PgBouncer in a compose profile (PR-DB-5).** Decisions: (1) *a profile and two variables, not a second compose file*: every URL's host and port are `${SMO_DB_HOST:-postgres}` and `${SMO_DB_PORT:-5432}`, so the default stack is untouched and the pooled one is one command; `migrate` stays direct because DDL and advisory locks want a session. (2) *Credentials*: each module has its own role, so PgBouncer needs every password; its entrypoint builds `userlist.txt` from the same compose secrets (SCRAM against plaintext on both sides) rather than an `auth_query`, which would need a SECURITY DEFINER function in the database; a rotation means restarting it. (3) *`options` is refused by a pooler*, and ignoring it would drop the statement and idle-in-transaction limits silently, so the services stop sending it behind a pooler and PgBouncer's `connect_query` sets the same two limits on every server connection. (4) *Prepared statements*: psycopg 3 prepares a statement after five repeats on one connection; behind transaction pooling the next transaction may land elsewhere. The safe default behind a pooler is off; `SMO_DB_PREPARE_THRESHOLD` turns it back on for a PgBouncer with `max_prepared_statements` (1.21+), which `scripts/pooler_check.py` shows working with the bundled one. (5) *The Helm chart gets no pooler*: the operator route of ADR 0003 brings its own (CloudNativePG `Pooler`), and the flag is the only thing the chart's modules need. The Alpine package was not available to read from the sandbox, so the image build is also first exercised in CI.
- **Staged CM jobs and the status constraints (revision 0026).** Found by the first test to put a staged job through Postgres (PR-HA-4.2): `POST /config-jobs` with `waveSize` returned 500 because `write_config_job_status_check` (from the baseline SQL) lists five states and `JobState` has six (`HALTED`, MGT-5, 0.2.0), and `write_config_sub_change_status_check` lacks `REVERTED` (MGT-5.5). Why it lived for two releases: SQLite (all unit tests) has no such constraint, the Postgres lanes ran single-wave jobs, and `check_migration_matches_models.py` compares columns, not checks. What changed: the revision; a Postgres test comparing the constraints with the code's states; the migration round-trip test's `_schema` now covers every schema and constraint *definitions* (it saw only `public` and constraint names since DB-2, so a revision touching a module's table was invisible to it). What this suggests for v0.5.0's validation work: every `CHECK (x IN (...))` in the schema should be compared with the enum or literal set that writes the column, not just these two; listed there (OPEN_ITEMS, v0.5.0). The reproduction took ten minutes against a local Postgres with the module's own role; the CI lane could not show the log of the failing pod, so the failing job step also keeps the pod logs now.

- **A1, the Near-RT RIC and E2 removed (release 0.5.0).** Decision: A1 policy management, xApps, the Near-RT RIC and E2 are out of scope at every stage (October 2026), so the `a1-related` module, `mock-near-rt-ric` and their wiring were deleted rather than frozen. What the entries above (§5 O-RAN-SC completeness, §7 spec audit, §9 waves, the PR-MSG-1.8 outbox conversion, PR-DB-2.7) say about A1 Related describes the code as it was in `smo-v0.4.0`. Choices: (1) *the four `a1_*` tables stay one release*: a rolling upgrade runs the previous release's code against the new schema, and that code uses them, so the revision that drops them comes after this release (`migrations/table_owners.json` lists them under `_retired` so the orphan-table checks stay honest); (2) *the role `smo_a1_related` is not dropped either*, for the same reason, and its entry left `db_roles.json` and the compose secrets, so nothing connects as it any more; (3) *the gateway answers `404 NO_ROUTE` for `/a1-related`* instead of a tombstone route, since a consumer of an A1 route would have to change anyway; (4) *the breaking-change check learned "removed-module"* (`scripts/check_breaking_changes.py`), so deleting a module's spec is a break that needs a reasoned waiver; (5) the demo runbook keeps its numbering, steps 11 and 22 become one-line notes. Not done: removing the "No A1, Near-RT RIC, xApp or E2" sentences of the sample rApps and call flows 22 to 25, which are true and say what the samples do not use.

### PR-OBS-3 — distributed traces (OBS-3.1, 3.2, 3.3, 3.5; 3.4 in part)

- **Two layers, so the default costs nothing (OBS-3.1 to 3.3).** `smo_shared/tracing.py`. (1) *Propagation, always on, standard library only*: a valid inbound `traceparent` (and `tracestate`) is parsed per the W3C rules (version 00, 32 and 16 hex ids, not all zero; version `ff` and a 00 header with extra fields are invalid; an invalid header is ignored, never an error), held in a context variable (like the correlation id and the originator, so the statelessness guard has nothing to flag) and sent on by `R1Client._call` and by the gateway, which drops the caller's header and sets its own. A request with no trace sends none: nothing is invented without a tracer. (2) *Spans, opt-in*: `SMO_OTEL_ENDPOINT` plus the SDK installed. The variable is this build's own and not the standard `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT` the open item named, because the standard one would also switch on FastAPI's native telemetry and any other OpenTelemetry-aware library in the image, and the switch here must mean exactly "this build's spans". A SERVER span per request (middleware installed by `apply_correlation_id`, inside the correlation middleware so the span carries `smo.correlation_id`), a CLIENT span per `R1Client` attempt and per gateway forward; the span becomes the new trace position, so the `traceparent` sent downstream names it. OTLP/HTTP through `BatchSpanProcessor`, `service.name` = `MODULE`, `ParentBased(TraceIdRatioBased(SMO_OTEL_SAMPLE_RATIO))`. Span names are the route template and the target module, never a path or query; nothing from bodies or headers is recorded.
- **Why spans are made by hand and not by the contrib instrumentation (OBS-3.4, not taken).** `opentelemetry-instrumentation-fastapi` / `-httpx` / `-sqlalchemy` would add more hashed packages for what two small context managers do, and would instrument every httpx call (the SME token fetch, webhooks) with the same weight as an R1 call. FastAPI 0.142, the pinned version, records its own server span once an SDK provider exists, which would give two server spans per request (found by the first span test); `apply_tracing` switches that off on the app (`app._telemetry["tracing"] = False`, a no-op on a FastAPI without it). Database spans (SQLAlchemy) are not done and stay open (OBS-3.4).
- **The packages are an optional build.** `requirements/tracing.in` / `tracing.txt` (hashed, constrained to `runtime.txt`, so no runtime pin moves), included in `dev.in` so CI runs the span tests, and installed into an image only by `--build-arg WITH_TRACING=1` (`SMO_WITH_TRACING` in compose); `smo-shared` has a `tracing` extra. The images the release workflow publishes are built without it: a published image given an endpoint logs one warning and propagates only. Whether to publish a tracing-enabled variant is open (OBS-3.7).
- **Logs.** `traceId` on every JSON log line of a request that belongs to a trace, and on the access line (read from the ASGI scope, set by the tracing middleware, because the access-log middleware runs outside it).
- **Compose and Helm (OBS-3.5).** Profile `tracing` (Tempo 2.8.2 on a volume, Grafana with Tempo and Loki provisioned and linked); `tracing.endpoint`, `tracing.sampleRatio` and `observability.tempo` / `.grafana` in the chart, the modules defaulting to `http://tempo:4318` when the bundled Tempo is on; the compose entry of every module passes `SMO_OTEL_ENDPOINT` (empty). One set of Tempo, Loki and Grafana files in `deploy/helm/smo/files/observability/`, mounted by compose and templated by Helm.
- **Tests.** `shared/tests/test_tracing.py`, the gateway's two tests, and the chart's tests (off by default, on renders, an explicit endpoint wins, the files parse and compose mounts them). The in-process hop test is an `R1Client` call from one app into another with spans off (the traceparent and tracestate arrive unchanged, none in means none out) and, with the SDK, the span tree across the hop. Not run: a live Tempo (OBS-3.6).

### PR-OBS-6 — log shipping (OBS-6.1, 6.2)

- **Fluent Bit to Loki, Grafana on top.** Compose profile `logging`: Loki 3.5.5 (filesystem, seven days), Fluent Bit 4.1.1 reading `/var/lib/docker/containers/*/*-json.log` (the Docker default driver), the `parser` filter unpacking the SMO's JSON line out of `log`, the `loki` output labelling streams with `service` and `level` only (and `job=smo`) so cardinality stays at modules times five levels; `correlationId`, `traceId`, `route` and `status` stay in the line and are filtered with `| json` at query time. Helm: the same as `observability.loki`, `.fluentBit` (a DaemonSet, `cri` parser, the release's namespace only, root with no capability and a read-only root filesystem, because the node's logs are root-readable) and `.grafana`, all off, on emptyDirs (a lab stack, said so in the values and the README). The Grafana data sources are provisioned from one file and link both ways (Loki's `traceId` is a derived field that opens the trace; Tempo's "logs for this span" runs the Loki query), so searching by correlation id and by trace id are in `docs/OBSERVABILITY.md` as LogQL.
- **Not taken, still open.** The Elasticsearch mapping is described in the document (field types), not shipped or tried (OBS-6.3). A live run of the profile is OBS-6.4 (see PR-OBS-3/6 live lane below). The log-volume budget is not done.

### PR-OBS-3/6 live lane (OBS-3.6, 6.4 written; first run pending)

- **Why.** Tempo, Loki, Fluent Bit and Grafana were never started in CI (the chart test only renders their values), so a Dependabot bump of any of their images (grafana 13, loki 3.7, tempo 3 and fluent-bit 5 were open at the time) could not be judged. The image pins are in two places, `docker-compose.yml` and `deploy/helm/smo/values.yaml` (`observability.*.image`); Dependabot bumps only the compose one, so the chart pin must be moved by hand to the version this lane proves.
- **What.** CI job `obs-stack` ("Tracing and logging profiles", `.github/workflows/smo-tests.yml`): `SMO_WITH_TRACING=1 SMO_OTEL_ENDPOINT=http://tempo:4318 docker compose --profile tracing --profile logging up -d --build`, then `docker compose exec -T r1-termination python3 - < scripts/obs_smoke.py`, the same way the other compose lanes drive their scripts. Logs of tempo, loki, fluent-bit and grafana are dumped on failure.
- **How the check works.** One `GET /bootstrap` through the gateway with a `traceparent` made by the script (a fresh trace id, sampled flag set). That one request yields both signals: the gateway's server span (found in Tempo at `/api/traces/<id>`, falling back to `/api/v2/traces/<id>`; the check counts spans and does not depend on the envelope or the id encoding) and its access log line, which carries `traceId` (found with the documented LogQL `{job="smo"} | json | traceId="<id>"`; the line itself must parse and carry the id). The request is made after Tempo and Loki are ready because Fluent Bit starts at the end of each file. Grafana: `/api/health` and `/api/datasources/uid/{tempo,loki}/health`. Every group retries to a deadline (120 s); output has statuses and the trace id, never headers or bodies. Standard library only, so it needs nothing installed in the gateway image.
- **Tests without docker.** `tests_integration/test_obs_smoke.py`: the pure functions (ids, span counting, readiness, Loki answer parsing, Grafana answers, the retry loop with a fake clock), the whole `main()` against a fake HTTP layer (healthy; each of trace, log and data source missing; a gateway that does not answer), and that the CI job names the script, both profiles and the four services' logs.
- **What the first run found (two defects, both fixed).** (1) Tempo 2.8.2 and Loki 3.5.5 never started: compose ran them as `user: "0"` with `cap_drop: [ALL]`, and root without `CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE` cannot write a named volume owned by the image's user 10001 (`mkdir /var/tempo/blocks: permission denied`, `mkdir /loki/chunks: permission denied`, a restart loop); the services now run as the image's own user. Fluent Bit did read the Docker logs and parse them; it only had no Loki to send to. (2) Grafana's Tempo data source has no backend health check (`GET /api/datasources/uid/tempo/health` answers 404 `plugin.notImplemented`; the UI tests it in the browser through the proxy), so the script calls Tempo's `/api/echo` through Grafana's data source proxy instead; Loki's data source keeps `/health`.
- **Not taken.** The configuration files were not changed for the new majors: no change could be shown to be needed without running them (see the pull request). The first CI run is what decides. OBS-3.6 and 6.4 stay open until the job has passed on `main`.

### PR-OPS-6 — GitOps example (OPS-6.1; 6.2 written, not synced)

- **Overlays render the chart; they do not patch its output.** `deploy/gitops/overlays/{lab,staging,prod}`: each is a `values.yaml` of only what differs plus a `kustomization.yaml` using the `helmCharts` generator with `chartHome` at `deploy/helm`, next to a base holding the namespace. Weighed against Kustomize patches over a rendered manifest: the chart already expresses every difference between environments as values, and a patch would tie an overlay to the chart's resource names. Secrets are named (`existingSecret`) and never in Git; hosts are placeholders. Two Argo CD Applications: the Helm source with an overlay's `values.yaml` as a value file (needs no `--enable-helm`; recommended) and the Kustomize overlay (needs `kustomize.buildOptions: --enable-helm`).
- **Tested:** `kustomize build --enable-helm` of all three overlays (Kustomize 5.7.1, Helm 3.18; the CI job `helm` runs it); `tests_integration/test_gitops.py` (YAML parses, referenced paths exist, every values key exists in the chart's `values.yaml`, no key named like a credential, the Argo CD `path` and `valueFiles` resolve). **Not done:** OPS-6.2's "syncs on a lab cluster": no Argo CD was available.

### PR-OBS-8 — /version per module, and readiness and version in the GUI

- **Route (OBS-8.1).** `smo_shared/health.py` `install_health` adds `GET /version` beside `/live`, `/ready` and `/health`, so every app that already installs the probes (the nineteen modules, the gateway and the four sample rApps included) gets it with no edit of its own `main.py`. The body is `{module, version, buildSha, builtAt}` (`VersionReport`), read from the environment on every call, not at import, so a test can set it: `MODULE` (the existing build argument; a sample rApp's `samples/` prefix is dropped; with none, the lower-cased app title), `SMO_VERSION`, `SMO_BUILD_SHA`, `SMO_BUILT_AT`, each `unknown` when absent or empty.
- **Image.** The shared `Dockerfile` declares the three as `ARG` with default `unknown` and turns them into `ENV`, as the last layers before `CMD`: a new commit invalidates only that layer, not the dependency and code layers. `docker-compose.yml` has an `x-build-info` anchor of `${SMO_VERSION:-unknown}`, `${SMO_BUILD_SHA:-unknown}` and `${SMO_BUILT_AT:-unknown}`, merged into the `args` of every build (`<<: *build_info`); `scripts/release_images.py` still reads `MODULE` from the same `args`. `release-images.yml` passes `SMO_VERSION` (the plan job's version), `SMO_BUILD_SHA` (the HEAD of the tag's checkout, not the workflow's `github.sha`, which on a manual dispatch is the dispatched branch) and `SMO_BUILT_AT` (UTC, ISO 8601) to `docker buildx build`; the main CI's `docker compose up --build` exports `SMO_BUILD_SHA` as the run's commit. A date is a build argument, so two builds of one commit differ in `builtAt` and are not byte-identical; that is accepted, since the image's digest is what a release pins.
- **Unauthenticated, like the probes.** The gateway's `public_paths` gains `/version` (its OpenAPI operation declares `security: []`), and so do `test_authz_walk.py`'s `PUBLIC_AT_THE_GATEWAY` and its "exactly the public ones" assertion; every backend's `/<module>/version` goes through the catch-all proxy and stays token-gated, which the same walk proves for all routes. `/version` joins `PROBE_PATHS` (access line at DEBUG, no request metric). `docs/openapi/*.json` regenerated: each of the twenty-one specs gains one `GET /version`; the breaking-change check reports no new break (an added path).
- **BFF (OBS-8.2).** `GET /api/modules/status` keeps its `/health` probe (and its `latencyMs`, which stays the health call's) and, for a module that is live, asks `/<module>/ready` and `/<module>/version` in parallel (the gateway's own through `R1Gateway.r1_get`, a public route needing no token). New per-module fields: `ready` (true for 200, false for 503, null when not asked or not answered with either), `version`, `buildSha`, `builtAt` (null unless `/version` answered 200). The probe never raises on the new routes: a timeout, a refused token or a 404 from an older release during a rolling upgrade are nulls, so the table keeps working while versions are mixed. Not taken: a `/version` of the BFF itself (it is not a row of the status table, and its CI deliberately has no `smo_shared`).
- **GUI (OBS-8.3).** `ModuleStatus` in `api/types.ts` carries the four fields; `moduleRows` in `lib/domain.ts` turns them into rows (DOWN, NOT READY, READY or UNKNOWN; version; the commit shortened to seven characters; build time; a dash for null and `unknown`) and flags a module whose commit differs from the most common one, which is how a rolling upgrade shows. The Dashboard's Module health card renders it with `DataTable` under the existing tiles. The Vitest suite includes only `*.test.ts` (no DOM renderer is configured), so the "component test" is on `moduleRows`, the logic of the component, in `lib/domain.test.ts`.
- **Tests.** `shared/tests/test_health.py` (the body, `unknown` when absent or empty, the `samples/` prefix, no readiness check consulted); `tests_integration/test_probes.py` (every app answers it; the gateway's operation is open); `gui-bff/tests/test_main.py` (ready and not-ready, a module without `/version`, a module that is down, the gateway's own routes, SME unreachable); four Vitest cases.
- **Not done:** a Prometheus `smo_build_info` gauge (the version is in `/version` and the GUI only); the Helm chart does not set the three variables (the images already carry them).

### PR-OPS-7 — the configuration reference

- **Generator (OPS-7.1).** `scripts/config_reference.py` parses (never imports) `<module>/app`, `samples/*/app`, `shared/smo_shared`, `sdk/smo_sdk`, `gui-bff/app` and the two scripts that run in the images (`migrate.py`, `db_roles.py`), tests excluded, and finds: `os.environ.get("X", d)`, `os.environ["X"]` (default `required`), `"X" in os.environ`, `os.getenv`, the same on a parameter named `environ` (the `def f(environ=os.environ)` testing seam used all over `smo_shared`), `environ.get("X") or d` (the `or` operand is the default), and `read_secret("X")` of `smo_shared/secretfile.py`, which yields two rows, `X` and `X_FILE`, both secret. Defaults are folded to a constant where the code allows it: literals, module-level constants (including a dict looked up by a constant key, so `DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS[kind]` gives each AIMgF timeout its own default), arithmetic, `str()` / `int()` / `float()`, f-strings of those. What cannot be folded is `computed in code`, and the description says how.
- **Wrappers are followed, not reported.** A function that reads the variable named by one of its own parameters (`_seconds(name, default)` in four files, `number(name, default)` inside `engine_options`, `_bool(name, default)` of the GUI settings, `settings_from_env(prefix)` with its closure and `f"{prefix}_MAX_BODY_BYTES"`) is recorded as a wrapper; each call site, in any walked file, is one variable with that call's own name and default. A wrapper whose default is not in the read itself takes its parameter called `default`. A name built from a function's return (`_cred_var(ref, part)`) is evaluated with the run-time parts as `<REF>`, so `NETCONF_CRED_<REF>_PASSWORD` is a pattern row. A name the walk cannot work out at all (the comprehension in `netconf_tls.tls_material_for`, the `for part` loop in `credential_configured`, the dict comprehension in AIMgF's timeout sweep) must be named by a `# config-ref: A, B` comment on its line or the line above, which replaces what the walk found on that statement; without one the read is `unresolved` and CI fails. Three such comments exist (`ran-nf-oam/app/netconf_ssh.py`, `netconf_tls.py`, `aimgf/app/main.py`), no behaviour change. The `${NAME:-default}` substitutions of `docker-compose.yml` are reads of the module `compose` (`SMO_DB_HOST`, `SMO_DB_PORT`, `PGBOUNCER_*`, `POSTGRES_SLOW_QUERY_MS`, the build arguments of `x-build-info`, and the GUI and R1 values it passes through).
- **Table (OPS-7.2).** 124 variables. `docs/CONFIGURATION.md` is prose (how to read it, the `*_FILE` convention, how compose and the Helm chart set a variable, what it does not cover) and, between two markers, the generated tables grouped by owner: `smo_shared` (read in the library, so by every module that uses that part), each module, "several modules" (the gateway's `*_URL` routing table, `SME_URL`, `SMO_ENROLLMENT_SECRET`) and "compose only". Columns: variable, default (several defaults when it is read in several places with different ones; a bare `.get("X")` next to one with a default in the same file is the same variable's guard and not listed), secret, files that read it (no line numbers: they move with every edit, and the document would be stale on each; `python scripts/config_reference.py` prints `file:line`), description. Secret is the name heuristic (PASSWORD, SECRET, KEY, TOKEN) or a `read_secret` read or its `_FILE` form; a description entry may override it with `{"description": ..., "secret": false}` (`IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_TTL_SECONDS`, `SME_TOKEN_AUDIENCE`, `GUI_INITIAL_PASSWORD_FILE`, `SMO_DB_ROLE_PASSWORD_DIR` are not secrets). The descriptions are `docs/config_descriptions.json`, keyed by variable name so regeneration keeps them; the generator renders a missing one as `TODO` and refuses to pass. All 124 are written, from reading the code.
- **CI (OPS-7.3).** `tests_integration/test_config_reference.py`: every variable read in code is in the table, the table lists none that no code reads, no unresolved read, every variable has a description and no description is orphaned, the documented default is the code default, the committed document is exactly what the generator writes, and secrets are marked. The extraction is exercised on a synthetic snippet (each way of reading, a wrapper with its own default, a prefix f-string, a run-time pattern, an annotation, a compose substitution), and the seeded miss runs a snippet with a new read through the same function: the real tree plus that one read is reported as undocumented, and a table with a row nothing reads is reported as stale.
- **Not taken:** reading `values.yaml` and the compose `environment:` blocks to add a "set in" column (the prose says where each deployment sets variables; a column would make the document change whenever compose does); a variable read through `kwargs` or `getattr(os.environ, ...)` is not seen (none exists); variables of third-party images (`POSTGRES_*`, PgBouncer's, nginx) are not the SMO's and are not listed.
### PR-STD — standards and compliance documents (STD-2.1, STD-4.1, STD-4.3, STD-5.1, STD-6.1)

- **The release table (STD-2.1).** `specs/README.md`, "Specification release table": one row per specification in `specs/` that `smo/` cites, and per spec the code cites that has no file (R1AP, SMO-ARCH, O2-DMS, TS 23.222, 32.300, 32.161, 28.552/28.554), with the release as the file states it. Decisions: (1) *the release is what the file says, nothing inferred*: the OpenAPI `info.version` of a 28-series file is the TS version it was generated from, so "Rel-19" is read from `19.x.y`, and the table says so; TS 29.222 and TS 29.482 name their TS version in `externalDocs` (V20.0.0 for five CAPIF files and V19.5.0 for one; V19.1.0), which is quoted; O-RAN YANG gets the range of its `revision` dates. (2) *no version is given for an O-RAN document that is not in `specs/`*: the repository records none, and the cell says "not recorded". (3) *the ~540 other 5G-core files are not listed*: nothing cites them. The table showed the releases are mixed (TS 28.105 at 19.5.0 while 28.104 and 28.312 are at 20.0.0, the Heartbeat file at 18.1.0, the 5GC NRM at 20.3.0), which `docs/STANDARDS.md` now says beside its matrices. **STD-2.2 not taken beyond a minimal list:** only RFC 7807 (obsoleted by RFC 9457) and RFC 7230 (by 9110/9112) are stated as newer, because they are certain from RFC numbering; every newer 3GPP or O-RAN release is "not assessed", and `STD-2.2` stays open. Found while reading `specs/README.md`: it still names `smo/SPEC_AUDIT.md` (merged into `OPEN_ITEMS.md`), lists A1 Related among the compared modules, and says no code references the spec paths (`scripts/ingest_yang_schema.py --library MnS/yang-models` does); left as they were.
- **The personal-data inventory (STD-4.1).** `docs/PRIVACY.md`, section 2: 18 rows from the models, migrations and code (every module's models were searched for person, subscriber and device identifiers). Findings that the inventory made and the rest of the repository had not said: (1) *no subscriber or UE identifier exists in any model*; the two columns with "subscriber" in the name are the SME invoker of an event subscription and a column nothing writes (`mdaf_report.subscriber_attribution`); what a real RAN puts in `pm_file.content` or an alarm text is stored as sent and is not assessed. (2) *The GUI audit log (`gui_audit_log`) is not hash-chained*: it is append-only because a `before_flush` listener refuses an ORM update or delete; the chain is `audit_log` (`smo_shared/audit.py`), which records gateway changes under the invoker id, and a GUI action reaches it as the GUI's own invoker, never the person. (3) *The GUI usernames are copied into module tables* as `smo-gui:<username>` (`write_config_job.requested_by`, `rapp_kill.killed_by`, `dme_action_record.requested_by`, `autonomy_dispatch.rejected_by`, the sample rApp's `override_by`) and as the plain name in `alarm.ack_user_id` / `clear_user_id`, by the `json_overrides` / `query_overrides` of `rbac.py`. (4) *`decided_by` (AI/ML model governance) and `pinnedBy` (host keys) are not pinned to the signed-in user*, unlike `requestedBy`. (5) *`scripts/db_backup.sh` does not back up the GUI's SQLite database* (users, GUI audit, counters), although the README said "every table". (6) *Retention is "none" for nearly every row*: the only deletions are SENT outbox rows (24 h), idempotency records (24 h), revocation rows, and the opt-in purges of CM snapshots and safeguard refusals; `PENDING` / `DEAD` outbox rows and `gui_login_failure` rows for names that never sign in are never removed. (7) *The GUI web server's default access log carries client IP addresses and the request path* (`DELETE /api/admin/users/<name>`); the application stores no IP. New items for what is not built: `STD-4.5` (erasure beyond the account), `STD-4.6` (pin the two fields), `STD-4.7` (tamper evidence for the GUI log), `DB-6.5` (back up the GUI database).
- **Erasure of a GUI user (STD-4.3).** `delete_user` now removes the user's `gui_login_failure` row in the same transaction as the account (it stayed before). There is no session row to remove: a session is a signed token naming the user, and `current_session` refuses a token whose user row is gone, so deleting the user ends every session on every instance; `gui_revoked_session` holds a token id and an expiry only, so it is neither personal data nor touched. *A defect found while writing the test*: a user created under a deleted name started at `token_version` 0 and so accepted the previous holder's unexpired token; `create_user` now starts a new user at `secrets.randbits(30)` (a token carries the version it was issued under, and the comparison is for equality). Seeded first-boot users keep 0. The test (`gui-bff/tests/test_main.py::test_erasing_a_gui_user_end_to_end_and_what_it_leaves_behind`) signs in by cookie and by script token, acknowledges an alarm through the proxy, ends a third session, fails two logins, deletes the user, and asserts what is gone (user row, counter row, both sessions, sign-in), that a re-created user does not revive either token, and what stays (every audit row naming the user, which the ORM still refuses to edit). **What remains and the options, written in `docs/PRIVACY.md` section 4:** the audit rows (append-only by design; on this log the constraint is policy and the ORM guard, since it has no chain) can be kept with a stated period (needs `DB-3.6`), pseudonymised at write time with an opaque per-user id, or rewritten by SQL under change control (possible only because the log is not chained, and it breaks the append-only claim; not recommended). The platform chain is a different matter and the document says so: a row cannot be edited or removed without `verify` failing, so a person's identifier must never be written there; today it is not (the actor is the GUI's invoker). *Not taken:* pseudonymising at write time (it changes what the audit log shows an admin and needs a schema change in the GUI database, whose tables are created by `create_all`, with no migration history), a route that rewrites module columns (a new write path into history), and deleting the audit rows (the log's purpose). The SQL for module columns is given as an example and marked untried. The audit chain format is unchanged.
- **The data-residency statement (STD-6.1).** `docs/DATA_RESIDENCY.md`: where data lives (Postgres; the SQLite file on `gui_bff_data`; `smo_packages`; `smo_scratch`; backups; logs; images) and every outbound path the code has: notifications to caller-registered URLs through `smo_shared.webhook` (literal loopback, link-local and reserved addresses refused; names are not resolved, and private ranges are allowed), the package download by location, NETCONF over SSH or TLS and RESTCONF to registered endpoints, the Kubernetes API with credential delivery on, and image pulls. *Checked rather than assumed:* there is no telemetry, analytics or update check in the code (no client for one; the GUI loads nothing external and its CSP is `self`); there is no outbound O2 call at all (FOCOM imports `httpx` and never calls it; NFO instantiates nothing on a cluster). The signature and provenance of an image are verified against the registry by the operator; nothing leaves a site for it.
- **The control matrix (STD-5.1).** `docs/CONTROL_MATRIX.md`: Annex A of ISO/IEC 27001:2022 by theme (organizational, with the technical controls mapped by their 8.x numbers; people and physical are "not applicable (software product)" with the reason) and the NESAS/SCAS test categories by name. Choices: (1) *conservative statuses*: "Implemented" only where a test or CI job is named; a control with a missing part is "Partial" with the item ID; what lives in GitHub settings (branch protection, required reviews, CodeQL's configuration, secret scanning) is "not verifiable from the repository" and not claimed. (2) *SCAS by category, not by requirement ID*: no SCAS exists for an SMO, and a mapping to TS 33.117 clause numbers would claim a conformance nobody tested; the matrix says it is not a NESAS audit. (3) *Written without the threat model*: `STD-5.1` was to need `SEC-14.2` (the STRIDE table), which is open; the matrix therefore maps what exists to the control themes and names "no threat model" as a gap (5.8, 8.26), and it should be revisited when `SEC-14.1`–`14.3` land. (4) *0.5.0 scope*: `OPEN_ITEMS.md` has no scope block for 0.5.0, so mTLS (`SEC-2`), OIDC (`SEC-6`), MFA and revocation (`SEC-7`), `/bootstrap` (`SEC-9`) and DR (`HA-6`) are cited by ID as Planned, as `docs/VALIDATION.md` V-7c already orders them before the external test. Stale statements found and corrected in `SECURITY.md` in the same change: the Python dependencies are a hashed lock, not unpinned; Dependabot is configured; the TLS profile exists; the Postgres password is generated, not `smo` / `smo`; the session key is stored once in the GUI database, not random per boot. Also noted, not changed: `OPEN_ITEMS.md` `SEC-7.4` still says only a `token_version` bump revokes a session, which has not been true since logout revocation (`gui_revoked_session`).

### PR-SEC-5 — Signing keys and token caching (SEC-5.1 to 5.3; 5.4 built, its load test open)

Decided for 0.6.0. The signed tokens in this build are the GUI session JWTs (SME's access tokens are opaque and introspected). Both halves are off by default, and with nothing set the behaviour is byte-for-byte the one before.

- **SEC-5.1, RS256 / ES256 for the session token.** `gui-bff/app/signing.py` (new) puts a signer behind `create_app`: `HmacSigner` is the existing `security.issue_jwt` / `decode_jwt` (HS256, header `{"alg":"HS256","typ":"JWT"}`, no `kid`, unchanged; it reads `cfg.jwt_secret` on every call because the lifespan replaces a generated secret with the stored one after the app is built), `AsymmetricSigner` signs with PyJWT (`pyjwt[crypto]`, already a locked direct dependency in `requirements/runtime.in` for the OIDC ID tokens, with `cryptography` 50.0.2 in the hashed lock: no dependency was added and the lock was not recompiled). Settings (`app/config.py`): `GUI_JWT_ALGORITHM` (`HS256` default, `RS256`, `ES256`; case and spaces ignored; anything else stops the start), `GUI_JWT_PRIVATE_KEY_FILE` (the file form only, the PEM of an unencrypted private key; RSA of at least 2048 bits for RS256, EC on P-256 for ES256), `GUI_JWT_PREVIOUS_KEY_FILES`. `build_signer` refuses at start what would otherwise be silently wrong: RS256 / ES256 without a key, a key file with HS256 (the operator would believe it is in use), the wrong key type, a small RSA key, another curve, an encrypted or malformed PEM, an unreadable file; the messages name the variable and never the key (tested). `main.py` calls `signer.issue` in `issue_session` (so the password login, the `/api/token` grant, the second step of the one-time-code login and the OIDC callback all follow) and `signer.decode` in `current_session` and `logout`. The login challenge of the one-time-code step stays an HS256 token under a key derived from `GUI_JWT_SECRET` (it never leaves the BFF), so `GUI_JWT_SECRET` is still needed under a key pair; tested that the challenge is not a session and that the one-time-code sign-in works under both algorithms.
- **What the verifier refuses.** The header `alg` must equal the configured algorithm (so `none`, `HS256` signed with the public key as a secret in three encodings, and the other asymmetric algorithm are refused before a key is touched); the key is looked up by `kid` in the configured set (a missing, unknown or non-string `kid` is refused, and `jwk`, `jku`, `x5c` in the header are ignored); the signature must verify; `exp` must be an integer in the future. PyJWT is called with `verify_exp`, `verify_iat` and `verify_nbf` off and the lifetime checked by the same rule as the HS256 verifier, so a replica whose clock is a second ahead does not refuse a session another replica just issued.
- **SEC-5.2, `kid` and a key set.** `kid` is the RFC 7638 thumbprint of the public key (tested against the RFC's example key). `GUI_JWT_PREVIOUS_KEY_FILES` lists earlier keys (comma separated; a public key is enough, a private one is used for its public half only); they verify and never sign; the same key listed twice or as the current key appears once. A token signed with the old key verifies while its public key is listed and is refused after it is removed (tested for both algorithms). Rotation procedure: `gui-bff/README.md` 2.11 and `docs/SECRETS.md`. **Sessions across a change of algorithm are not carried over, on purpose:** an HS256 session is refused by an RS256 / ES256 BFF and the reverse, though the BFF still holds `GUI_JWT_SECRET`. Accepting old HS256 tokens beside the key pair would keep the shared secret as a live way to forge a session, which is the thing moving to a key pair removes; the price is one sign-in per user. Tested both ways.
- **SEC-5.3, `GET /.well-known/jwks.json`.** On the BFF, outside `/api`, no session and no CSRF (public by design; it is the public half). `200 application/json`, `Cache-Control: public, max-age=300` (the security-header middleware only fills headers that are not set), `{"keys": []}` under HS256 and never the secret; under a key pair each key has `kty`, `use: sig`, `alg`, `kid` and the public members only (`n`, `e` or `crv`, `x`, `y`; tested that no private member is present). `gui/nginx.conf` forwards that one exact path (`location =`) to the BFF; the other `/` locations are unchanged. **What the step's text assumed that is not so here:** the BFF has no committed OpenAPI file and is not covered by `tests_integration/test_contract_schemathesis.py` or by the route walk of `test_authz_walk.py` (that walks the backends behind R1 Termination, where the "unauthenticated route" rule is checked); the BFF's permission table (`rbac.py`) is for the proxied `/api/smo/...` routes only. So no spec was regenerated and no walk expectation changed; the route is covered by `gui-bff/tests/test_signing.py` (status, content type, cache header, members, and a walk of the BFF's GET routes asserting that, with no session, the only ones that do not answer 401 are this and the three that were already public). The route is documented in the route's own `responses` (it appears in `/api/openapi.json`).
- **Deployment.** Compose passes `GUI_JWT_ALGORITHM`, `GUI_JWT_PRIVATE_KEY_FILE`, `GUI_JWT_PREVIOUS_KEY_FILES` (all empty or HS256 by default; the file is mounted by an override, as for `GUI_TOTP_KEY_FILE`). The chart has `gui.jwtKeySecretRef` (`{name, key}`, key default `private-key.pem`), modelled on `totpKeySecretRef` except that a key is a file: the whole Secret is mounted read-only at `/run/gui-jwt` in the `gui-bff` pod (`defaultMode: 0440`, readable by the pod's group, as the mTLS Secret) and `GUI_JWT_PRIVATE_KEY_FILE` is set to the path of the named key; previous keys go in the same Secret and are named in `gui.env`. `docs/SECRETS.md` has the row and the rotation procedure; `docs/config_descriptions.json` and `docs/CONFIGURATION.md` the three variables (the two path variables are marked not secret).
- **SEC-5.4 ★, the introspection cache at R1 Termination.** `r1-termination/app/introspection_cache.py` (new) and `main._introspect_token`. `R1_INTROSPECTION_CACHE_SECONDS` (default 0 = off, read on every call; a bad or negative value leaves it off with a warning), `R1_INTROSPECTION_CACHE_MAX_ENTRIES` (default 10000, read once). The key is the SHA-256 of the token (tested that the raw token is nowhere in the cache object); a positive answer is held for `min(TTL, exp - now)` from SME's `exp` (never past the token's end), a negative one for `min(TTL, 5)` s, an SME failure never; the oldest entry goes when full, expired ones first. **Revocation:** SME has no per-token revocation; a token stops being valid when its invoker is offboarded (`DELETE /invoker-registrations/{id}`) or purged. The gateway's `proxy` evicts after a 2xx answer to `DELETE /sme/invoker-registrations/{id}` (that invoker's entries) and to `POST /sme/invoker-registrations/purge-stale` (everything), so the replica the revocation went through refuses the token on the next request; a generation counter makes an introspection that was in flight during the eviction not store its (possibly pre-revocation) answer. **The bound, stated honestly:** through the same replica, immediate; through another replica or at SME directly, at most `R1_INTROSPECTION_CACHE_SECONDS` after the revocation (tested at 29.9 s and 30.1 s with a 30 s TTL); the role SME records for an invoker is seen with the same delay. **SME down:** an entry inside its TTL is served without asking SME (a short outage does not stop callers whose token was checked recently), the first request after it expires gets 503 `AUTH_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE` with `Retry-After`, a stale entry is never served, an unknown token is a 503 (tested). Metric `smo_introspection_cache_total{result="hit"|"miss"}` in `smo_shared/metrics.py` (not one of the mutation-tested modules; nothing is counted while the cache is off). Compose and chart pass both settings (`0`, `10000`).
- **Default off, and why.** The owner trades the instant effect of a revocation for fewer SME calls; a small default would change an upgrade's security behaviour without anyone choosing it. The first rApp token lasts an hour, so even a 30 s window is small against it, but that is the owner's call (`OPEN_ITEMS.md`, SEC-5.5).
- **Not done: the load test (done later: PR-SEC-5.4b).** "Load test shows fewer SME calls" needs `scripts/load_run.py` against the compose stack, which was not available. What exists is a unit test with a fake SME that counts introspections (ten requests with two tokens cost two SME calls). No latency or CPU effect is claimed. Left in `OPEN_ITEMS.md`.
- **Not taken.** For the keys: a key generated and stored in the BFF database; an inline PEM in the environment; encrypted PEMs; EdDSA and the SHA-384 / SHA-512 variants; `x5c` / `x5t` in the key set; the BFF fetching its own key set; scheduled rotation; one key set mixing algorithms; carrying HS256 sessions over a switch. For the cache: a cache shared between replicas (Redis, or the database: it would put back the round trip the cache removes), a revocation broadcast between replicas, a default above 0, and a global (not per token) negative cache.
- **Tests.** `gui-bff/tests/test_signing.py` (33), `r1-termination/tests/test_introspection_cache.py` (25). Existing tests unchanged. Not run here (no Docker, no helm, no Postgres): `docker compose config`, `helm lint` and the chart render (`test_helm_chart.py` skips its render tests without helm), the contract suite with a live stack, a browser sign-in under a key pair.

### PR-SEC-6 — OIDC login for the GUI (SEC-6.1 to 6.7)

- **What was built.** `gui-bff/app/oidc.py` (the relying party, no web framework in it), the routes in `app/main.py` (`GET /api/auth/config`, `GET /api/oidc/login`, `GET /api/oidc/callback`), the table `gui_oidc_login` (`app/db.py`), the settings in `app/config.py` (14 `GUI_*` variables, in `docs/CONFIGURATION.md`), the sign-in button and the error line in the SPA (`gui/src/pages/Login.tsx`, `src/lib/oidc.ts`). Off by default; with it off nothing changes and the three routes answer 404 or report `enabled: false`. `gui-bff/README.md` section 2.9 is the description of the flow; this entry is the record of the choices.
- **SEC-6.1, configuration validated at start.** `OidcConfig.from_settings` runs when `create_app` builds the app and raises `ValueError` naming the variable: a missing issuer, client id or redirect URI; an issuer, redirect URI or post-logout URI that is not an absolute https URL (http only for localhost, or with `GUI_OIDC_ALLOW_HTTP`, which exists for the CI Keycloak on the compose network); `openid` missing from the scopes; a role that is not viewer, operator or admin in the group map; an entry with no group; no map and no default role ("nobody could sign in"). The client's credential is `GUI_OIDC_CLIENT_SECRET` or the file named by `GUI_OIDC_CLIENT_SECRET_FILE`; both set is an error. The settings object holds it as `oidc_client_credential` (a neutral name, because CodeQL's taint heuristics flag secret-named values).
- **SEC-6.2, authorization code with PKCE (S256), state and nonce kept in the database, not in a process.** The sign-in in flight is a `gui_oidc_login` row (`state` primary key, `nonce`, the PKCE `verifier`, `binding_hash`, `expires_at`, ten minutes), created by the login route and consumed by the callback with a `DELETE` whose row count decides who got it, so a replay, an expired state and two instances racing all find nothing, and a callback may land on another instance than the redirect (tested with two `Database` objects on one file). *A design choice beyond the step:* the state is also tied to the browser that started the sign-in, by a random value in the cookie `smo_oidc` (HttpOnly, `SameSite=Lax` because the provider's redirect back is a cross-site navigation that `Strict` would not carry, `Path=/api/oidc`, ten minutes) of which only the SHA-256 is stored; without it an attacker could start a sign-in at the provider as themselves and get a victim's browser to open the callback URL (login CSRF). The table has no personal data. It is not an Alembic revision: every `gui_*` table lives in the BFF's own database and is made by `Base.metadata.create_all` on start (the other tables there are in no migration either, and none is in `table_owners.json` or `db_roles.json`), so an existing database gets the new table when the new image starts and the previous release's code never reads it. Unauthenticated, the login route inserts a row, so it removes expired rows each time and refuses (`too_many_logins`) when 5000 are waiting.
- **SEC-6.3, ID-token validation.** PyJWT does the signature and the registered claims, with the algorithm taken from an allowed list of asymmetric ones (RS/PS/ES 256, 384, 512), so `alg=none` and the HS256-with-the-public-key confusion are refused before any key is looked at; the key is the JWKS entry with the token's `kid` (RSA and EC, `use` absent or `sig`). Checked: `iss` equals the configured issuer, `aud` contains the client id, `azp` equals it when present or when there are several audiences, `exp`, `iat`, `sub`, `iss`, `aud` required with 30 s leeway, `nonce` equal to the one stored (constant-time), `at_hash` against the access token when the token has one. A token is never validated without a nonce. Discovery and the JWKS are fetched with `httpx` and an explicit timeout (`GUI_OIDC_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`), not PyJWT's own client, so the timeout guard (`tests_integration/test_http_timeouts.py`) holds and a test can substitute the transport. Both are cached in the `OidcClient` of the app for an hour (a cache of public provider metadata, so the statelessness guard has nothing to list: it is an instance attribute, not module state); an unknown `kid` makes one refetch, at most once in 30 s, so a forged `kid` cannot make the BFF hammer the provider; an unreachable provider leaves the last good copy in use; the discovery document's `issuer` must equal the configured one and its endpoints must be https. PyJWT was already in the image (`pyjwt[crypto]` in `requirements/runtime.txt`, used by SME); `gui-bff/pyproject.toml` now lists it.
- **SEC-6.4, groups to roles.** `GUI_OIDC_GROUPS_CLAIM` (default `groups`; a list, or a string split on spaces and commas; a dotted name reaches into an object, so `realm_access.roles` works) is looked up in `GUI_OIDC_GROUP_ROLE_MAP` (`group=role,...`); the highest role matched wins (`rbac.RANK`); no match gives `GUI_OIDC_DEFAULT_ROLE`, empty by default, and empty is a refusal (`no_role`): a person who is not in a mapped group never gets a user row. Only the ID token is read: the userinfo endpoint is not called (one less dependency and a smaller PII copy; the cost is that the provider must put the groups in the ID token, which for Keycloak is a group-membership mapper, as in `gui-bff/tests/keycloak/smo-realm.json`).
- **SEC-6.5, user on first sign-in.** `gui_user` row `oidc:<subject>`: the colon is outside `USERNAME_RE`, so no local account can ever have such a name; `password_hash` is `!`, which `verify_password` rejects (no `salt:digest`), so there is no password and the row cannot be used at `/api/login` (tested with `!`, an empty string and an arbitrary one); a random starting `token_version` as for any created user. The role is set from the token at every sign-in (a hand-set role lasts until the next one, and the audit row says `role a->b`); a disabled user is refused (`account_disabled`); a person who signs in with no mapped group has an existing row's `token_version` bumped, ending their earlier sessions (otherwise a person removed at the provider would keep the session they had until it expired). An admin cannot set a password on such a user (409 `OIDC_USER`). Not taken: a readable name from `preferred_username` or the e-mail. `gui_user` is keyed by name, the schema does not change in a minor release, and a name the person controls at the provider is not an identity (it can be reused or changed), so the subject is the name; the audit row names the issuer. Confirm with the owner whether the Users page should show a display name (needs a nullable column, a later revision).
- **Session, audit.** The 303 to `/` carries the same `smo_session` and `smo_csrf` cookies, from the same `issue_session`, so CSRF double submit, `jti` revocation, `token_version` and "role read from the row on every request" apply unchanged. Audit actions `OIDC_LOGIN` (username `oidc:<subject>`, role, `iss=<issuer>` and `created`, `existing` or `role a->b`) and `OIDC_LOGIN_FAILED` (the subject when the token was valid enough to have one; detail a reason code with a validation class name or claim name, never a token, a code or text from the provider). The browser is told only the reason code (`/login?oidc_error=<code>`, nine codes) and the SPA maps each to its own sentence, and an unknown code to a generic one, so nothing from the provider or the URL is rendered.
- **SEC-6.6, logout and end-session.** Logout revokes the session as before. For an `oidc:` user, when the discovery document has an `end_session_endpoint`, `POST /api/logout` also returns `endSessionUrl` (`client_id`, and `post_logout_redirect_uri` when `GUI_OIDC_POST_LOGOUT_REDIRECT_URI` is set) and the SPA navigates there (RP-initiated logout, http(s) only). No `id_token_hint`: the BFF keeps no ID token (a second place for personal data and a token to protect), so the provider may show a confirmation page; Keycloak does for a request with only `client_id`. Not taken: back-channel logout.
- **SEC-6.7, break-glass.** `GUI_LOCAL_LOGIN_ENABLED` (default `true`). `false` answers `/api/login` and the `/api/token` password grant 403 and the SPA hides the form; the start is refused when it is `false` and OIDC is off. The seeded `admin` is untouched either way, so turning the flag back on restores the break-glass path.
- **MFA.** Not built natively (`SEC-7.1` to `7.3` stay open "only if required"): with OIDC on, the second factor is the provider's policy; the local break-glass login has none, which `docs/CONTROL_MATRIX.md` says.
- **Tests.** `gui-bff/tests/test_oidc.py` (68): a fake provider on `httpx.MockTransport` with an RSA key generated in the test, checking the redirect URI, the client credentials and the PKCE verifier like a real one; the flow, roles and users, and the negative cases (unknown, replayed, expired state; a callback from another browser; wrong or missing nonce; wrong audience and `azp`; wrong issuer; expired token; missing claims; tampered payload; another key's signature; `alg=none`; HS256 keyed with the public key; malformed token; wrong `at_hash`; provider errors not echoed; refused code and PKCE verifier; unknown group denied), keys and discovery (cache, rotation, forged `kid`, unreachable provider, wrong issuer), logout, the local-login flag, twelve bad configurations, and a sign-in finished on a second instance. `gui/src/lib/oidc.test.ts` (the message mapping and the login link guard). The browser check against a real Keycloak is `scripts/gui_oidc_e2e.py`, job `oidc` of `.github/workflows/smo-gui-e2e.yml`, realm `gui-bff/tests/keycloak/smo-realm.json` (client `smo-gui` with S256 PKCE, a group-membership mapper, three groups and four throwaway users); it was written without being run (no Docker daemon, no registry access and no browser in the authoring environment), so the first CI run is its first run.
- **Still open.** SEC-6.8 (LDAP bind, optional) and the items in `OPEN_ITEMS.md` SEC-6.9: a second provider, back-channel logout, `id_token_hint`, a run against a provider other than Keycloak. The Helm chart passes the settings through `gui.env` and the client's credential through `gui.oidcClientSecretRef`; it was not rendered here (no `helm`).

### PR-SEC-7 — logout revocation (SEC-7.4)

- **Logout ends the session itself (SEC-7.4, done in V-13c).** Every GUI session token carries an id (`jti`); `POST /api/logout` records it in `gui_revoked_session` (primary key `jti`, `expires_at` indexed; created with the other BFF tables by `Base.metadata.create_all`, entries removed once the token would have expired anyway) and `current_session` answers `401 SESSION_REVOKED` for a recorded id, for the cookie and for a Bearer token, and across replicas because the table is shared. A design choice not taken: a row per live session (the step's wording), because a revocation list stays small (only logged-out sessions, purged at expiry) while a session table grows with every login. A token issued before the change has no id and lives until it expires. Tests: `gui-bff/tests/test_main.py` (three) and the browser check `scripts/gui_session_e2e.py`. SEC-7.5 (an admin action to revoke all of a user's sessions) is open; a password change or deactivation already bumps `token_version`.

### PR-SEC-7 — MFA (SEC-7.1 to 7.3, 7.5 to 7.8)

Decided for 0.6.0: both layers. (1) A setting that makes the identity provider the only login, so its MFA applies. (2) Native one-time-code (TOTP, RFC 6238) login for local accounts, mandatory for the admin role when asked, and the login of the break-glass account. Nothing changes by default.

- **SEC-7.1, the secret and the enrolment.** Table `gui_user_totp` (`username` PK, `secret_enc`, `confirmed`, `last_step`, `created_at`, `confirmed_at`), mapped in `gui-bff/app/db.py`. *There is no Alembic revision:* the BFF's own database is not the SMO's Postgres schema and its tables, `gui_*`, are made by `Base.metadata.create_all` (README 2.2). A table that is new is made at start; the one column added to a table that exists, `gui_user.break_glass` (boolean, `NOT NULL DEFAULT false`), is added by `Database._add_missing_columns` (an `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN` when the column is absent, tolerant of a second instance adding it at the same moment), expand-only so the previous release's code, which neither reads nor writes it, keeps working on the upgraded file (tested: a database made before gains the column, the old insert still works). `tests_integration/test_migrations.py` and `HEAD` are therefore unchanged. The secret is 160 random bits, base32. It is stored with AES-256-GCM (`cryptography`, already locked through `pyjwt[crypto]`: no dependency added) under a key from `GUI_TOTP_KEY` / `GUI_TOTP_KEY_FILE` derived with HKDF-SHA256 (one derivation per purpose), a random nonce each time and the user name as associated data. The key is read in `gui-bff/app/config.py` with the file-or-value rule of the OIDC client credential, **not** through `smo_shared.secretfile`: the BFF's code does not import `smo_shared` (its CI job does not install it; the same reason `_client_credential` and `_enrollment_secret` repeat the rule). The key must be at least 32 characters; without one enrolment is refused (503 `TOTP_UNAVAILABLE`), and an account that is already enrolled fails closed (503 `TOTP_KEY_UNAVAILABLE`, no session). TOTP itself is `gui-bff/app/totp.py` on the standard library: HMAC-SHA1, 30 s step, 6 digits, a window of one step either side compared in constant time, replay refused by an atomic `UPDATE gui_user_totp SET last_step=:s WHERE last_step < :s` (so a code is good once, across instances; the code that confirmed the enrolment counts as used). `POST /api/me/totp/begin` returns the base32 secret and the `otpauth://` URI once and stores the secret *not active*; `POST /api/me/totp/confirm` needs a valid code from it and only then activates it. A second enrolment needs an admin's reset; an `oidc:` user cannot enrol (the provider asks). *Not taken: a QR image* (no QR-rendering dependency; the page shows the key in groups of four and the link).
- **SEC-7.2, the second step.** `POST /api/login` (the route's real path; the task text said `/api/auth/login`, which does not exist: `/api/auth/config` is the only route under `/api/auth`) with a correct password for an account with a confirmed code opens no session and sets no cookie. It returns `{mfaRequired: true, challenge, expiresIn: 300}`: an HS256 token of kind `login-totp` (user, `ver`, random `jti`) signed with a key derived from the session key by HMAC, so it is never a valid session token and no session token is a valid challenge (both tested), plus a `gui_login_challenge` row that makes it single use (a `DELETE` whose row count decides, on any instance). `POST /api/login/totp` checks the signature, the kind, the five minutes, the row, that the user is still active with the same `token_version` (a password change or "revoke sessions" in between ends the sign-in) and the mode rules again, then the code; only the right one spends the challenge and opens the session. A wrong code leaves the challenge usable and counts in the same `gui_login_failure` counter as a wrong password. *A defect avoided:* the password step used to clear that counter on success; with a second step that would give a guesser who knows the password a fresh budget of code guesses per password round (tested: 4 rounds of 2 guesses give five 401s and then 429). The counter is now cleared only by a finished sign-in. This lockout is the rate limit of the step; a limiter per client address on the login routes is `SEC-8.6`, still open. *The password grant:* `POST /api/token` would have been a way round the second factor, so for an account with a code it needs the form field `otp` (a code or a recovery code), and a code typed to confirm an enrolment or to make new recovery codes counts towards the same lockout.
- **SEC-7.3, recovery codes.** Ten, shown once in the confirmation answer. 16 characters of a 31-symbol alphabet without i, l, o, 0, 1 (about 79 bits), in four groups. Stored in `gui_recovery_code` as HMAC-SHA256 under a key derived from `GUI_TOTP_KEY`, over the user name and the code. *Why not scrypt like the passwords:* a slow hash protects a secret that can be guessed offline, and 79 random bits cannot be, so scrypt would only cost the CPU of up to ten hashes at every recovery sign-in; the key means a copy of the database alone cannot even be tested against guesses. A code is spent by an `UPDATE ... WHERE used_at IS NULL` whose row count decides; it is audited (`RECOVERY_CODE_USED`, detail "n left") and the answer carries `recoveryCodesLeft`, which the page shows. `POST /api/me/totp/recovery-codes` (needs a current one-time code, not a recovery code) replaces the set.
- **SEC-7.5, admin actions.** `POST /api/admin/users/{username}/revoke-sessions` bumps the user's `token_version` (that is how logout-independent revocation works: every session token, cookie or Bearer, carries `ver`; `jti` revocation is per session and logout's), so every session of the user ends on every instance, and a half-done sign-in ends too (audit `USER_SESSIONS_REVOKED`). `POST /api/admin/users/{username}/reset-totp` removes the secret, the recovery codes and the open challenges (audit `TOTP_RESET`); sessions stay (the page says to revoke them too if the device may be in other hands). Both use `require_admin` like the rest of `/api/admin`. *`rbac.py` is the permission table of the proxy only* (`/api/smo/...`); the BFF's own routes have never had entries there, and adding them would add rules `decide()` is never asked about and that `test_rbac_matrix.py` would then test against nothing. The V-13b check for them is `tests/test_mfa.py::test_every_admin_route_is_admin_only`, generated from the app's routes (each method of each path under `/api/admin`: 401 without a session, 403 for viewer and operator), so a new admin route is covered the day it is added. Admin page: a "One-time code" column, a Break-glass checkbox, "Revoke sessions" and "Reset one-time code" buttons.
- **SEC-7.6, `GUI_LOGIN_MODE`** (`both` default, `oidc`, `local`; anything else stops the start). `oidc`: the password is still checked first (a wrong one is the same 401, counted), and a correct one for an account that is not break-glass is then refused, 403 `LOGIN_MODE_OIDC_ONLY` with the provider's name in the detail and an audit row `LOGIN_REFUSED`; refusing before the check would have made the answer depend on whether the name is a break-glass account, which is a way to find them. `/api/token` answers 403 in the same case. The start is refused when the mode is `oidc` and OIDC is not configured. `GET /api/auth/config` (the existing public route the sign-in page reads) now also says `loginMode` and `breakGlass`, and `localLogin` is false in mode `oidc`: the page shows only "Sign in with <provider>", with a small "Break-glass sign-in" link that opens the form. `local`: no OIDC client is built (a leftover provider setting cannot stop the start), `/api/oidc/*` answer 404 and the config says `oidc.enabled: false`. `GUI_LOCAL_LOGIN_ENABLED=false` still wins over everything: with it off there is no break-glass login either.
- **SEC-7.7, break-glass.** `gui_user.break_glass`, set by an admin (`PATCH /api/admin/users/{name}` `{breakGlass}`; refused for an `oidc:` user). A break-glass account signs in with password and one-time code in any mode, and cannot sign in without an enrolled code (403 `BREAK_GLASS_NEEDS_TOTP`, so the order is: flag it, enrol its code, then rely on it). Every break-glass sign-in (cookie, recovery code or token grant) is audited as `BREAK_GLASS_LOGIN`, never `LOGIN`, and logged at WARNING with the user and role (never a secret); the flag is checked again at the second step. The OPEN_ITEMS text said "raises an event": the audit row and the warning are the event here (no event bus exists in the BFF).
- **SEC-7.8, `GUI_ADMIN_MFA_REQUIRED`** (default off; needs a key or the start fails). `current_session` answers 403 `MFA_ENROLMENT_REQUIRED` for every route but `GET /api/me` and `/api/me/totp[/begin|/confirm]` to a local admin without a confirmed code (one read of `gui_user_totp` per request of such an admin; Bearer tokens too); `/api/me` and the login answer carry `mfaEnrolmentRequired`, and the SPA redirects to Account security and shows nothing else in the sidebar. The sign-in itself is not refused (the admin must be able to sign in to enrol). An `oidc:` admin is not asked. The "on in the production sample" of the item moves to `DB-3.10`, which creates that sample.
- **GUI.** `LoginCodeStep` (the second step, accepts a code or a recovery code, strips spaces and dashes), `TotpEnrolment` and the page `Security` (`/security`, every role: the key and link, the confirmation, the recovery codes shown once with "I have saved them", new codes), the sign-in page (break-glass link, mode-aware), the Admin additions, a redirect for `mfaEnrolmentRequired` (`lib/mfa.ts`, `mustEnrol`), `describeError` reads the `{detail: {title}}` shape of errors raised from the session dependency, and the page list of the browser check gains `/security`. Vitest: 32 new tests (10 in `lib/mfa.test.ts`, 6 for the code step, 7 for the enrolment page, 8 for the sign-in page, 1 in `client.test.ts`) (`src/testing/dom.tsx` is a 50-line jsdom helper; the repository has no testing library, and `vite.config.ts` now includes `*.test.tsx`).
- **Configuration.** `GUI_LOGIN_MODE`, `GUI_TOTP_KEY`, `GUI_TOTP_KEY_FILE`, `GUI_TOTP_ISSUER`, `GUI_ADMIN_MFA_REQUIRED` in `docs/config_descriptions.json` and `docs/CONFIGURATION.md`; compose passes the first, the key, the issuer and the last (the key is *not* a compose secret file and `scripts/init_secrets.sh` does not make one: a new required file would stop an existing stack that has not re-run it, and the feature is opt-in); the chart passes them through `gui.env` and takes the key from a Secret of the operator's (`gui.totpKeySecretRef`). `docs/SECRETS.md`, `docs/PRIVACY.md` (rows 20 to 22, the erasure table), `docs/CONTROL_MATRIX.md` 8.5, `docs/PENTEST_SCOPE.md` updated.
- **Tests.** `gui-bff/tests/test_mfa.py` (62): the RFC 6238 and RFC 4226 vectors, the window and the replay; the secret not in clear in the row; the challenge (expiry, single use, other user, not a session); recovery codes; lockout shared with passwords; the mode; break-glass; the admin gate; revoke and reset; deletion of a user; no secret, code, recovery code, challenge or key in the log (DEBUG) or any audit row; the old-database column. Existing tests changed: the two exact `auth/config` bodies in `test_oidc.py` gained `loginMode` and `breakGlass`. The unit suite is 946 passed.
- **Not taken.** WebAuthn / FIDO2 (a larger piece with a browser API and attestation; TOTP is the baseline and a security key can sit beside it); SMS or e-mail codes (a weaker factor, and a delivery dependency); a QR image; "remember this device"; self-service removal of the code (it would let a stolen session remove the second factor); re-encrypting stored secrets under a new key (a key change is a reset of every user's code, `docs/SECRETS.md`); an extension of `scripts/gui_session_e2e.py` for the second step (it could not be run here, `docs/VALIDATION.md` V-13c, "Not yet"); a limiter per client address on the login routes (`SEC-8.6`).

### PR-SEC-2 — mutual TLS between services (SEC-2.1, 2.2, 2.3, 2.5); SEC-3 decided

- **What was built (SEC-2.1, 2.2, 2.3).** Opt in by `SMO_MTLS=on`, off by default (every call and every start is as before; a test pins that the default compose file does not mention it). `smo_shared/mtls.py` is the one place: the file paths (`SMO_MTLS_CERT_FILE`, `_KEY_FILE`, `_CA_FILE`, default `/run/mtls/...`), `http_url()` (the `http://` default address of a peer, the gateway's route table, the SME audience, `R1_GATEWAY_URL`, the sample rApps' and RAN NF OAM's own callback addresses, becomes `https://`), `client_kwargs()` (an `ssl` context with the module's certificate loaded and the CA as the only root, TLS 1.2 minimum, cached by the files' modification times so a renewed file is used by the next call), `uvicorn_args()` and `probe()`. Server side: the `Dockerfile` command takes `python -m smo_shared.mtls uvicorn-args` and appends `--ssl-certfile --ssl-keyfile --ssl-ca-certs --ssl-cert-reqs 2`; if that command fails (mTLS on, a file missing or empty) the container exits, so a missing certificate never means plain HTTP (`tests_integration/test_mtls.py` runs the real command line for both). Client side: `R1Client` (bootstrap, invoker registration and offboarding, the grant, every module call), the gateway's `httpx.AsyncClient` for proxying and for the token introspection at SME, and the GUI backend (its own copy of the few lines: it does not install `smo_shared`). `smo_shared.webhook` presents the certificate only to an `https://` destination inside the deployment (single-label name, `*.svc`, `*.svc.cluster.local`, `SMO_MTLS_INTERNAL_HOSTS`): a caller-supplied destination is untrusted input, so the module's certificate is never offered to it and a public destination is verified against the system roots.
- **Certificates (SEC-2.1).** `scripts/mtls_certs.py` (the `cryptography` package, no `openssl` needed): `init` makes a CA (ECDSA P-256, 10 years, `pathlen:0`) and a certificate for each of 23 names (20 servers, the RAN NF OAM worker, the GUI backend, and `runbook` for the replay), 90 days, serverAuth and clientAuth for a server, clientAuth only for a client-only name; the CA key is 0600 in a 0700 directory and never inside a service's directory; `client NAME` makes a certificate for an operator or an external rApp; `renew`, `rotate-ca` and `status` below. One certificate per service serves as both server and client certificate, the simplest thing that gives each service its own identity (two per service doubled the files for no extra property today).
- **Compose (SEC-2.2, 2.3).** `docker-compose.mtls.yml` (an override, so the default stack is untouched): each service bind-mounts its own directory read-only at `/run/mtls`, sets `SMO_MTLS=on`, and swaps its healthcheck for `python -m smo_shared.mtls probe /ready`; the GUI backend also sets `SMO_MTLS_SERVE=off`. Bind mounts rather than compose secrets: 23 certificates and keys as secrets would be 47 secret definitions for the same effect, and the directory is the unit a rotation rewrites.
- **Probes.** A server that requires a client certificate cannot be probed by a plain HTTP request, so the probe runs in the container and presents the service's own certificate (compose `healthcheck`, chart exec probes). Choices not taken: a second plain port (an unauthenticated listener on every service, defeating the point), a probe-only client certificate (the kubelet would have to hold a key), and `CERT_OPTIONAL` (a service would then answer a client with no certificate).
- **Identity binding: not used.** The certificate carries the module's name (CN and SAN), but uvicorn does not give the peer certificate to the application, and the gateway's decisions already rest on the introspected token and the role SME records; the token check is unchanged (`scripts/compose_mtls_check.py` asserts that a valid certificate without a token is still 401). Binding a certificate name to the invoker, and a caller allow-list from it, stays open as `SEC-3.3`.
- **Rotation (SEC-2.5).** Leaf certificates: `renew` (same CA), then a rolling restart; a client picks a renewed file up on its next call, a server loads its files at start. The CA, without a moment at which two services disagree: `rotate-ca trust` (every `ca.crt` holds old and new), `issue` (new leaves from the new CA; the bundle still holds both), `retire` (new CA only), a rolling restart after each; `tests_integration/test_mtls.py` handshakes every pair of services that are one phase apart, both directions, and shows the old leaf refused after `retire`. The CI job `compose-mtls` renews and recreates three services in turn while the gateway keeps answering, then repeats the refusal checks. For the chart: cert-manager renews at `renewBefore` and rewrites the Secret; a restart (or a reloader) puts it into a server. Expiry: `smo_mtls_cert_not_after_timestamp_seconds{file}` per module (read from the files at scrape time, exported only with mTLS on), the alerts `SmoMtlsCertExpiring` (under 14 days, warning) and `SmoMtlsCertExpiryImminent` (under 3 days, critical), each with a runbook page (`OBS-7.5`: written like the other pages, and like them not yet replayed by hand). `docs/runbooks/README.md` no longer lists certificate expiry as uncovered, except the edge's own (the deployment's).
- **Chart.** `mtls.enabled` (default false); per-module `mtls: server | client | off` (the mock adaptor and the GUI's nginx `off`, the GUI backend `client`, a worker `client`); each participant mounts the Secret `<module>-mtls` (`tls.crt`, `tls.key`, `ca.crt`, the keys cert-manager writes) at `/run/mtls`, with exec probes for a server. `mtls.certManager.enabled` renders the Certificates (and, with `createCA`, a self-signed Issuer, a CA Certificate and a CA Issuer; or an `issuerRef` of the operator's); without it the Secrets are the operator's, made from their own CA or from `mtls_certs.py` output. `mtls.ingressClientSecret` adds the ingress-nginx annotations that make the ingress speak HTTPS to the gateway with a client certificate. **Not run**: the sandbox has no `helm` binary, so none of the chart's render tests (`test_mtls.py`, gated on `helm`) has run; CI's `helm` job is their first run.
- **Proof, and what was not run.** `shared/tests/test_mtls.py`: off changes nothing, fail closed, the context reloads on a changed file, a real uvicorn built from the printed options answers a client with a certificate and refuses one with none, one from another CA, and plain HTTP on the TLS port, `R1Client` against it, the probe, the expiry metric. `gui-bff/tests/test_mtls.py`. `tests_integration/test_mtls.py`: the script, the rotation phases, the compose override against the script and the chart, the image command. CI job `compose-mtls`: the stack up with the override, every healthcheck green over TLS, `scripts/compose_mtls_check.py` (each of 20 services: right certificate answered; none, another CA's, and plain HTTP refused; the plain services still plain; `/bootstrap` advertises `https://`; the token gate intact), the runbook replayed over mTLS by a client holding the `runbook` certificate (`tests_integration/live.py` makes the mesh https; the notification destinations in `test_demo_runbook.py` are `https://` in this mode), and the rotation. **The compose stack could not be started in the sandbox (no Docker daemon)**: the replay and the compose checks are untried until that job runs; a callback path the replay does not exercise and that still uses an `http://` address of another module would fail under mTLS and is the likeliest finding.
- **SEC-3 decided: no service mesh.** The owner's decision is native mTLS ("will go with mTLS"), so `SEC-3.1` (sidecar injection documentation) and `SEC-3.2` (strict mesh policy manifests) are not built and not documented; an operator who runs Istio or Linkerd leaves `SMO_MTLS` off, and `docs/RELEASES.md` criterion 6 is met by `SEC-2` alone. `SEC-3.3`, the caller allow-list, stays open.
- **Not taken, still open.** `SEC-2.4` (Postgres `verify-full`: the compose and bundled chart Postgres serve no TLS; it needs a server certificate and `sslmode` in the URL; built later: PR-SEC-2.4 below). The compose `tls` edge with the override (nginx would need `proxy_ssl_certificate`). Hot reload of a server's certificate (a restart is the procedure). A published rApp port outside the stack that cannot hold a client certificate (use the chart's `mtls.ingressClientSecret` and an edge that terminates the rApp's TLS).

### PR-OPS-9 — sizing from a measured load (OPS-9.1, 9.2)

- **Measured, not guessed.** `scripts/sample_stats.sh` samples `docker stats` during the load run and `scripts/sizing_report.py` turns the samples into per-container peak memory, median and p95 CPU and a suggested request and limit (`.github/workflows/smo-load.yml` prints the table in the job log and summary). The run behind the chart's values: 20 callers for 120 s against 10 000 seeded elements, 38.9 requests/s, p95 682 ms, no errors (`docs/SIZING.md`).
- **What it showed.** The gateway is CPU-bound at one core (about 26 ms of CPU per request); SME is the largest module (361 MiB); the other modules peak at 75 to 135 MiB and are nearly idle; Postgres 150 MiB.
