- **Only CI can show.** The compose network (the platform reaching the stub at `http://r1-termination:8898`, Onboarding fetching the package from it, the gateway's guard accepting that base), the `docker compose exec` plumbing of the step, and Playwright's own Chromium (not the one used here).
- **Not taken.** Running the stub as its own compose service (it is a test double: a service in the product's compose file would ship it); using a real sample rApp (it needs its model and data pipeline, which is what its own demo is for); a screenshot comparison.

### PR-SEC-5.5 — the introspection cache default: 30 seconds (decided by the owner)

The owner accepted the recommendation recorded in `PR-SEC-5.4b`. **What changed:** `R1_INTROSPECTION_CACHE_SECONDS` is `30` in `docker-compose.yml` (`${R1_INTROSPECTION_CACHE_SECONDS:-30}`) and in the chart (`modules.r1-termination.env`); `.env.example`, `r1-termination/README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, the configuration reference and `OPEN_ITEMS.md` say so. **What did not:** the code's own default (nothing set) stays `0`, so a test, a script or a deployment outside compose and the chart that sets nothing behaves as before, and `0` turns the cache off everywhere.

- **The cost, in the owner's words accepted.** A token revoked at SME, a re-scoped invoker or a changed role is honoured for up to 30 s longer on every gateway replica except the one that carried the change. Offboarding an rApp, the kill switch and a role change reach the gateway through SME or the kill-switch table: the kill switch reads RAN NF OAM's `rapp_kill` table on its own path and is not delayed by the cache (`r1-termination/README.md`, "Introspection cache").
- **Evidence.** One token on one machine, then the CI numbers (`smo-load.yml`, `docs/PERFORMANCE.md`).
- **Not taken.** A revocation broadcast between replicas (the window would be zero); open in `OPEN_ITEMS.md` `SEC-5.5`.

### PR-AI-11 and PR-AI-13 follow-ups — two-person approval, retention, the samples' `decision`; SB-7.8, the VES password from a Secret

The four gaps the 0.7.0 report named, in one change. The rule for all four: **opt in, nothing changes for an existing user until they act.** Revision `0034` only (expand only, parent `0033`).

- **Two-person approval (AI-11, a second approver).** A policy row (`rapp_approval_policy`) and an instance's `approvalPolicy` take `requiredApprovals` 1 or 2; the default is 1, which is the code path of 0.7.0 untouched (every new branch is guarded by `required_approvals > 1`, and a test pins that a request that needs one approval answers as before: `ran-nf-oam/tests/test_approvals.py` and `test_decision_records.py` pass unchanged). The number is copied onto the request when it is parked, like `on_timeout`, so changing the policy never changes what a waiting request needs. The approvals so far are a JSON list on the request (`approvals`: `by`, `at`, `reason`), changed only under the `FOR UPDATE` lock every decision already takes, so two simultaneous approvals by two people serialise and two by the same person cannot both count; a separate vote table with a unique key was weighed and not taken (a second table, its owners entry and its purge for a list of at most two). **State: no new status.** The first approval leaves the request `PENDING` (the status CHECK of `0031` is untouched, which an extra `PARTIALLY_APPROVED` would have needed to widen, and every consumer of `status=PENDING`, the inbox above all, keeps working); `approvals` says how far it is. The first approval is recorded and answers 200 with `jobStatus` null: it runs no check and writes nothing, because the checks that matter run at the approval that writes, as they do for one approval (a kill switch thrown between the two approvals stops the job at the second). Everything the single approval does (safeguards again, scope, limits, MSAC, schema, dispatch, the decision record, the audit chain) happens at the second approval, by the same code.
- **Who counts.** *Different* is the `decidedBy` string compared without case or surrounding space (`smo-gui:Alice` and `smo-gui:alice ` are one person); the GUI backend pins it to the signed-in user, so a browser cannot choose it. The requester's own approval never counts: the existing check (`decidedBy` equal to the invoker id or `requestedBy`, or a call carrying the requester's own id) is kept as it was, and for a two-approval request it also holds for the same names differing in case or space. **This is only as strong as `decidedBy`.** RAN NF OAM is internal-only at the gateway and trusts the name the GUI backend sends, as it always did; a caller that reaches it directly can send two names. The platform has no stable person identity below the GUI user name (local users and OIDC users share one namespace of names). Recorded as a decision for the owner, not hidden. One rejection, by anyone but the requester, ends a two-approval request whoever has approved; a person who approved may still reject. A timeout after one approval lapses it (EXPIRED or REJECTED by the policy it was parked under); the approval is kept in `approvals` and the record.
- **A refusal at the second approval keeps both.** `_execute_write` can refuse after the vote was set on the row (a safeguard commits its own refusal record, which commits the vote with it; MSAC and the schema check do not, and the rollback undoes it). `_close_refused` therefore adds the vote only if it is not already in the list; the first version of this change added it twice for a safeguard refusal and the test for that case (`test_a_safeguard_that_refuses_at_the_second_approval...`) failed on it, which is how it was found.
- **The record names both.** `rapp_decision_record.approvers` (nullable JSON) holds the approvers in order for a two-approval request (an empty list when it ended before any approval) and NULL for every other record. **The hash:** `content_hash` covers `approvers` only when it is not NULL, so no existing record, and no record of a single approval, hashes differently (a test pins that NULL and an empty list differ and that a changed list is a `MISMATCH`). The record's `approvedBy` is the last approver; `approvers` is the whole answer. The audit row carries the hash, as before.
- **API and wire.** `PUT /rapp-approval-policy` and the instance's `approvalPolicy` take the field (`Literal[1, 2]`: 422 otherwise). The policy view and rApp Management's stored policy show `requiredApprovals` only when it is 2, and rApp Management pushes it only then: an instance that did not ask for it stores, shows and pushes exactly what it did (the existing tests compare those dicts exactly and pass unchanged). The request views gain `requiredApprovals` and `approvals` (additive; for a single approval that is approved, `approvals` shows the one approver, so the GUI renders both the same way). The approve route answers `jobStatus: null` for a first approval. The notice (`docs/NOTIFICATIONS.md`) gains `requiredApprovals` and `approvalsGiven` for a two-approval request only. **Fail closed across versions:** rApp Management accepts a push of a two-approval policy only if RAN NF OAM's answer says `requiredApprovals: 2`; a RAN NF OAM of the previous release ignores the field and would hold the instance's writes for one approval, so the instance stays `DEPLOYING` (503) like any refused push. **What this does not cover:** a request already parked as a two-approval request and then decided by an old replica during a rolling upgrade (it ignores the column and treats one approval as the decision); the changelog tells the operator to set the field only when every replica runs this release. Roles are unchanged: operators and admins decide, an admin sets the policy, no new route. The new error code `APPROVAL_ALREADY_GIVEN` (409) is defined in `ran-nf-oam/app/main.py`, not in `smo_shared.errors`, so the mutation-tested module is untouched.
- **GUI.** The Approvals list has an *Approvals* column (*1 of 2 approvals*, or *one needed*); the drawer lists who has approved, says whether the signed-in user's approval would be the first or the last, disables Approve for a person who has already approved (they can still reject) and words the toast so a first approval does not claim a write. The Safeguards dialog has *Approvals needed* (default one; it sends `requiredApprovals` only when two is chosen). The Decisions list and record show both approvers.
- **Retention (AI-11 / AI-13, DB-3 mechanism).** Two settings, `SMO_RETENTION_APPROVALS_DAYS` and `SMO_RETENTION_DECISION_RECORDS_DAYS` (days, `0` or unset keeps, the DB-3 reading of `retention_days`), and two tasks in `ran-nf-oam/app/tasks.py` beside the other three purges (`purge-approvals`, `purge-decision-records`, hourly, in the single-runner worker of MSG-4, batches of 1000 through `smo_shared.retention.purge`, each also calling `report_retention_off`). **Approvals** are purged by `decided_at` and only when the status is not `PENDING` (a waiting request has no `decided_at`, and the status is named as well). **Decision records** are purged by `occurred_at` and only when `audit_seq` is set: a record not yet written to the chain is never deleted, because the chain row is the surviving evidence. **No `audit_log` row is ever deleted** (the purge is a `DELETE` on the two RAN NF OAM tables only; a test compares every chain row's seq and hash before and after and runs `audit.verify`). **The earlier argument for never purging** (HISTORY above: a removed decision record is not a tamper signal the chain can show) is answered, not ignored: the chain row for a purged record stays, so the chain is whole and verifies, and what is lost is the record's text (rationale, approvers, inputs reference), which was never in the chain; `GET /decision-records/{id}` is a 404 afterwards. That is why the proposed value in `docs/RETENTION.md` is *keep*, why the production sample does not set either variable, and why the document says to export the list first if the text matters. Defaults `0` in code, compose (`:-0`) and the chart's `ran-nf-oam-worker` env; `docs/config_descriptions.json` and the generated `docs/CONFIGURATION.md`; `.env.example` lists both commented at `0`. `tests_integration/test_retention_samples.py` gained a `KEPT` set so the table's *keep* rows are checked against the samples (not set), compose, the chart and `.env.example` (`# VAR=0`). Not taken: a signed marker so a purge is itself evidenced in the chain (the open item in `RETENTION.md` for the chain itself), a purge route for a scheduler (the worker is the way, as for alarms), and purging by status (a rejected request earlier than an approved one).
- **The samples' `decision` (AI-13).** The Python SDK exposes it (`Platform.execute_action(decision=...)`, `Data.mediate_action(decision=...)`). Each sample's `_execute_direct` (the restore path: wake, revert, rollback, operator override) now sends `{inputsRef: "<rApp>:<instance>:execution:<execution id>", modelVersion: <the instance's model_version>, rationale: "Restoring service: <reason>"}`, and Mobility Optimization's DMRO-bounds write at deploy sends a model version and a rationale. References and text only, as the platform's `DecisionContext` says. The four `.csar` files are rebuilt (their sources changed). **Not covered:** the writes an AUTONOMOUS rApp's intent makes through the Intent Service and the SA SMOS handler carry no `decision` (the context would have to travel in the dispatch and each expectation, a change to two other modules' contracts); the sample READMEs say so. **Go and Java:** neither SDK has a call for DME actions or config jobs (`sdk-go` has `Client.Do` and the typed helpers for data, models, platform registration and the instance's own routes; `sdk-java` has the equivalent clients), and neither example rApp writes configuration, so there is nothing to send and they are unchanged.
- **SB-7.8, the VES password from a Secret.** Chart value `ranNfOam.vesPasswordSecretRef: {name, key}` (default `{}`), modelled on `gui.jwtKeySecretRef`: when named, the `ran-nf-oam` pod (and no other) gets one volume from that Secret with only the named key (`items`, default key `ves-password`) mounted read-only at `/run/ves` (`defaultMode: 0440`, readable through `fsGroup`), and `RAN_NF_OAM_VES_PASSWORD_FILE=/run/ves/password`. Empty: nothing is rendered (a test renders every Deployment and checks no `RAN_NF_OAM_VES*` variable and no volume). **It turns the listener on** (the listener exists only with a password), so it is opt in by nature; the README says so. Setting the value and also `RAN_NF_OAM_VES_PASSWORD` or `_FILE` in `modules.ran-nf-oam.env` fails `helm template` with a message (the listener answers 503 for a password given twice, so it would be a silent outage). The README gives the adaptors' network note (the gateway does not route `/ves/...`; the chart's NetworkPolicy covers R1 Termination only; with mTLS on, RAN NF OAM wants a client certificate an O1 adaptor will not have). Tested by `tests_integration/test_helm_chart.py` with `helm`. Compose has no VES variables (it never passed any), so nothing changed there.
- **Not taken.** (1) Approver groups, a role required per approval, more than two approvals, per-region approvers. (2) A notice for the first of two approvals (the second approver is told by the request notice and sees the count). (3) A `PARTIALLY_APPROVED` status. (4) A separate vote table. (5) Purging the audit chain, or evidencing a purge in it. (6) A purge by status or per rApp. (7) `decision` through the intent path. (8) A person identity below the GUI user name.
- **Verified here / only in CI.** Here (Postgres 16): `ruff`, `scripts/run_mypy.sh` for `ran-nf-oam`, `rapp-mgmt`, `gui-bff` and the four samples, the `ran-nf-oam` suite (coverage 98.1 against a floor of 97), `rapp-mgmt`, `gui-bff`, the four samples, `tests_integration` with `SMO_TEST_POSTGRES_URL` set, the migration round trip for `0034` (up, down one, up; the CHECK refuses 3; `check_migration_matches_models.py` 138 tables), the regenerated OpenAPI specs and contract tests, Vitest, typecheck and build of the GUI, and `helm lint` and the rendered tests. Only CI: the previous-release lanes for `0034`, the browser check, two replicas deciding one two-approval request at once (the row lock is the one a single approval takes; SQLite ignores it), and the retention purges against Postgres at volume.

### PR-GUI-9a — the operator console redesign ("Signal"), built from the design hand-off

Built from the hand-off kept in `gui/docs/redesign/` (BRIEF, SCALE, STRUCTURE, one mockup per page). What remains is `PR-GUI-9` in `OPEN_ITEMS.md`.

- **Theme.** `gui/src/styles.css` carries the hand-off's tokens: a dark default, a light theme, five accents (`data-accent`), four text sizes as the root font size (`data-size`; every font size is in rem), reduce-motion. Older class names (`.badge.tone-*`, `.btn.primary`, `.stat`, `.tab.active`, `.sev-critical`…) are kept as aliases, so a rApp's declared page (OperatorUi) and existing tests still theme correctly. Fonts come from `@fontsource/*` and are served by the GUI: the CSP's `font-src 'self'` stays. `public/theme-boot.js` (a file, because the CSP forbids inline script) applies the browser copy of the preferences before React, so there is no flash of the wrong theme. Four light-theme status colours and two light accent text colours are darker than the hand-off's values, and red badge text on its soft fill in the dark theme is lighter (`--bad-ink`): the hand-off's values measured 3.7–4.5:1 against the soft fills on raised grounds. With the change, axe (WCAG 2 AA) found no serious or critical violation on any page in either theme, run here against the built GUI with a faked `/api`.
- **Shell.** `gui/src/shell/`: a 248 px sidebar grouped Overview / Automation / Network / Account with SVG icons, badges (critical + major alarms, waiting approvals, halted config jobs and campaigns), the environment chip (modules healthy) and the user card; a 64 px top bar with breadcrumb, a ⌘K jump box, notifications, the preferences button and help. One navigation table (`shell/nav.ts`) feeds the sidebar, the breadcrumb and the jump box.
- **Kit.** `gui/src/kit/`: Kpi, Meter, Segmented, Badge, Callout, Diff, Timeline/Steps/MiniSteps, the explicit states, SectionBoundary, Pager, ServerTable, icons; `components/charts.tsx` gained StackedBars, RingGauge and LineChart (thinned past 300 points).
- **Scale fixes (SCALE.md P1, P2, P8, P10).** Lists page on the server (`kit/ServerTable`, "Showing 1–50 of N"), which ends the silent cut at the backend's default 100 rows. Counts come from a new BFF route, `GET /api/summary/{page}` (`gui-bff/app/summary.py`): each is the owning module's `total` for a one-row page with the defining filter, fanned out in parallel, cached 5 s and shared by every user, with one computation per page at a time; a module that does not answer leaves its counts `null` and is named in `partial`. An action refreshes only the reads of its own module and of the modules `data/keys.ts` lists for it, plus the counts, instead of every read. Every section has loading, empty and error-with-retry states and its own error boundary. Pages but the Dashboard and sign-in are loaded on first use (the first bundle went from 855 kB to 343 kB).
- **Preferences.** `GET`/`PUT /api/me/preferences` (`gui-bff/app/preferences.py`, table `gui_user_preference`, made by `create_all`, removed with the user), every field a closed set and unknown fields refused, because theme, accent and size are written onto `<html>`. The page `/preferences` previews unsaved changes on itself only.
- **Pages.** Every page became a folder (`index.tsx`, `sections/`, `data/queries.ts`, a `README.md` maintenance sheet, `__tests__/`), keeping every route, action and role check; each page README lists its sections, calls and known limits. New: RAN topology, Configuration (config jobs, vendors and schemas, endpoint trust, element onboarding), Software (campaigns with a required dry run), Element detail (config history and diff, managed-object tree loaded on expand, cell guards); AI/ML Registry and governance history, Intents utility formulas, KPIs MDA functions/requests/report files, Admin RAN access control, Infrastructure Topology and O-Cloud inventory levels. Lifecycle flows have one board per flow at `/flows/<id>`; `lib/flows.ts` gained flows 15, 16 and 19 and flow 02's three phases (covering 17 and 26).
- **Checks.** GUI: 38 test files, 360 tests (from 16 and 210), typecheck, build, diagrams. BFF: `tests/test_preferences_summary.py` (15) and the whole suite (999). `scripts/gui_e2e.py` covers the new pages and checks four pages in both themes with every accent; the CI browser scripts' hooks (the "Username"/"Password" labels, "Sign in", "Operator Console", "Modules healthy n/m", "rApp directory", "Search rApps", "☆ Pin") were kept.
- **Not taken.** The hand-off's palette applied unchanged (it fails AA on soft fills, above); a server-side search behind the ⌘K box (it would have to be a new BFF fan-out; the box says it jumps, not searches); building the back-end asks of SCALE.md §5 or filling the data gaps of BRIEF §5 (both are `PR-GUI-9`; the widgets show "—" or are left out, and nothing on screen is invented); "approve similar for 1 hour" (a proposal in the hand-off, not a backend feature).

### PR-GUI-9b — the back end the console redesign asked for, and the console wired to it (GUI-9.1, 9.2, 9.4, 9.5, 9.6, 9.7, 9.8, 9.9)

Closed from `OPEN_ITEMS.md` (what is left stays there as GUI-9.3, 9.5b and 9.8b).

- **Live updates (9.1).** `GET /api/events` (gui-bff `app/events.py`): Server-Sent Events, one shared poller per BFF process recomputing the subscribed summary pages every 5 s through the summary cache and pushing only changed counts, a ping every 15 s, the session re-checked at each ping, at most 4 topics per stream and 5 streams per user per instance, a stream ending itself after 30 minutes; the GUI's nginx passes it unbuffered. The GUI (`src/data/events.ts`, `shell/LiveEvents.tsx`) opens one stream per tab for the sidebar and the visible page, writes the counts into its cache, refetches only the lists whose counts changed, backs off on reconnect, and drops its polling to 60 s while connected ("Live · pushed"); the alarm table shows "N new alarms — show", and a critical alarm can beep (a preference). The poller is on the statelessness allowlist with its reason.
- **Search (9.2).** `GET /api/search`: a parallel typeahead over elements (ran-nf-oam `search`), rApps (the BFF's directory), alarms, models and decisions, 2 s per source, cached 10 s per query and role; the ⌘K box shows the groups.
- **Counts on the servers (9.4).** ran-nf-oam: alarm filters (`ack_state`, `open_only`, `probable_cause`, `since`/`until`, `region`), `/alarms/counts` (by severity, ack state, cause, element, region, and 24 hourly buckets by severity, in SQL), `/alarms/stats` (mean time to acknowledge from a new `acknowledged_at`), `/alarms/{id}/correlated` (same element within a window, server-side), topology link paging, a `reciprocal` filter and `/topology/links/counts`; aimgf `/model-lifecycles/counts`. The alarm table now filters only on the server (keyset-paged), and the topology and AI/ML boards read these counts.
- **Paging and export (9.5).** Keyset `after` cursors on alarms, decision records and the BFF audit log; streamed CSV exports of decision records (`since` required, at most 31 days) and the audit log (admin, itself audited), with formula-looking cells neutralised.
- **Global stop (9.6).** rapp-mgmt `PUT|DELETE|GET /kill-all`, reusing the per-instance stop (an instance already stopped keeps its author and reason); internal-only at R1 (`smo_shared/roles.py`), so no rApp can stop every rApp.
- **Permission rules (9.7).** Thirteen BFF rules for routes the console showed read-only: negotiation feedback, MDA requests, KPI check, managed-object refresh and kill-all PUT (operator); kill-all DELETE, SSH host keys (with `pinnedBy` forced to the GUI user), MSAC writes and the site cluster (admin).
- **Data the design needed (9.8).** Element search and `site_cluster` (migration 0036), a health map with a network health score (`100 × elements without an open critical or major alarm / elements`) and a worst-element ranking; the newest KPI per rApp, single and batched; training epoch, total and ETA; intent `fulfilmentPercent`, `fulfilled`, `inConflict` with list filters; DME `expectedIntervalSeconds`, `lastDeliveryAt` and `late` (migration 0037, backfilled); sign-in history, users' last activity and used recovery-code slots in the BFF. Not built, because no module records it: node utilisation (FOCOM, README §2.8) and an rApp's per-cell history.
- **Browser checks and screenshots (9.9).** Run here against the full compose stack in Docker (images built locally through the session proxy; the OS package upgrade steps were skipped in that local build only): `scripts/gui_e2e.py` (18 pages, and four pages in both themes with every accent: 0 serious or critical axe findings), `gui_rapp_pages_e2e.py`, `gui_session_e2e.py`, `gui_smoke.py` all passed, and the four sample rApp demos ran end to end on the new back end. The 167 screenshots under `gui/docs/screenshots` were recaptured from a freshly seeded stack by the new `scripts/gui_screenshots.py` (lifecycle states driven through the GUI, another party's part through the module APIs as DEMO_RUNBOOK does; 0 failures, about 16 minutes).
- **Not taken.** A WebSocket (SSE is one-way, which is all the console needs, and passes proxies as plain HTTP); a search index (the fan-out reads each module's own filters, bounded and cached); an asynchronous export job queue (streaming with a hard bound covers the audited use; GUI-9.5b).

### PR-GUI-9c — scope, background exports, node utilisation and the rApp's cell history (GUI-9.3, GUI-9.5b, GUI-9.8b)

Closed from `OPEN_ITEMS.md`. Its two follow-ups, GUI-9.10 and GUI-9.11, are closed in PR-GUI-9d.

- **Scope (9.3).** RAN NF OAM filters every element-tied list by `region` and `site_cluster` in SQL (alarms and their counts and stats, decision records and their export, config jobs, cell guards, topology links and counts, software campaigns, O1 endpoints, element onboarding, approvals, the health map and the worst elements), only ever narrowing the caller's ADR 0005 claim; a multi-element row (a decision, an approval, a campaign) is in a place when any of its elements is. `GET /managed-entities/scopes` feeds the picker. rApp Management filters instances by their `authzScope` region (unrestricted instances included by default). Safeguard refusals record no element and stay fleet-wide. The BFF passes the scope to the counts whose route takes it, keys its cache and its event topics by it, and names the counts it could not narrow; the GUI keeps the scope in the URL, applies it in one place (`api/hooks.ts`) and marks a box "network-wide" when the scope cannot narrow it.
- **Background exports (9.5b).** `POST /api/exports` starts a decisions (operator) or audit (admin) CSV job in the BFF instance that accepted it, paging the source by keyset and storing the file in chunks in the BFF database, so any instance on a shared database serves the download; a heartbeat lets another instance mark an interrupted job FAILED; 24 h expiry, 3 running jobs per user, 10,000,000 rows; requested, downloaded and deleted are audited. The GUI's Decisions and audit pages create jobs, and `/exports` lists them. The Decisions table pages by keyset.
- **Data (9.8b).** FOCOM serves node utilisation from `CPU_UTILIZATION` / `MEMORY_UTILIZATION` performance records (this build's dictionary entries: O2 IMS defines none; 0–100 % checked on ingest; migration 0038 adds the index), single and batched; the runbook has the producer step, and the screenshot walk-through runs it. The Energy Saving rApp serves its cells' state history from its own decision trail and declares a chart and a table panel for it (kinds the renderer already draws), so the history belongs to the rApp, as an rApp's data should. The Dashboard's "Needs your attention" is one cached BFF call (`/api/summary/attention`, also an event topic).
- **Not taken.** Scoping refusals (a migration to record elements on every refusal; refusals are per rApp, and the page already filters by rApp); a job queue service for exports (an in-process task with a database heartbeat covers a restart without a new component); a time-to-scale chart axis for the rApp history (the renderer's axis is categorical; changing it would change every declared chart).

### PR-GUI-9d — the Dashboard in three calls; GPU utilisation not needed (GUI-9.11, GUI-9.10)

Closed from `OPEN_ITEMS.md`.

- **The Dashboard in three calls (9.11).** `GET /api/summary/dashboard` carries `panels` (gui-bff `app/summary.py` `PANELS`): the latest six
  decisions (`/decision-records?limit=6&total=false`, the page envelope), the fleet health by region (`/managed-entities/health?group_by=region`),
  the ten worst elements (`/managed-entities/worst?limit=10`) and the 24 hourly alarm buckets (`/alarms/counts?group_by=hour`), each asked in the
  same parallel fan-out as the counts, narrowed by the scope (`SCOPED_PATHS` gains the three aggregates), checked with `decide` like a count, and
  cached 5 s for every user with them. A panel whose module failed is null and its module named in `partial`. The event stream compares the
  panels with the counts (`panel.<name>` in `changed`) and sends them with the counts, so a new decision or a changed health group is pushed.
  The GUI's Dashboard hooks (`pages/dashboard/data/queries.ts` `usePanel`) read the panels; the first load is the summary, the attention groups
  and the shell's module status, pinned by the Dashboard test. The site-cluster drill-down and the trends still load when opened.
- **Not taken.** Folding the attention groups into the summary too (two calls): they have their own topic and event, pushed separately, and
  their rows are larger; the module status is the shell's read on every page, not the Dashboard's. A panel per page for every list (the other
  pages' lists are server-paged tables the operator pages through; a panel is for a small, fixed answer). The direct reads stay as the fallback
  for a BFF without panels, so a GUI and a BFF of different builds still work during a rolling upgrade.
- **GPU utilisation (9.10), closed as not needed.** Every O-Cloud node of this deployment is CPU only, so there is nothing to measure; FOCOM's
  utilisation dictionary (`CPU_UTILIZATION`, `MEMORY_UTILIZATION`) stays as it is. A GPU-capable O-Cloud would add a `GPU_UTILIZATION` entry and
  a producer that sends it, the same way.

### PR-GUI-10 — the GUI review findings (GUI-10.1 … GUI-10.10)

A review of the console listed thirteen defects; three were already gone with the redesign (the Safeguards dialogs inside a `<tr>`, the Dashboard counting a first page with a hard-coded module total, a test title that disagreed with its assertion). The other ten are fixed, each with a test: BFF rules for creating and deleting FM subscriptions and deleting PM subscriptions, so the form and both Unsubscribe buttons appear for operators; the package drawer says what Onboarding reports (verified against its trust store, or accepted unsigned) instead of "verified (dev cert)"; a blank or whitespace-only "Regression allowed, %" and a whitespace-only declared number are refused or left out instead of read as 0; the role matrix is computed from the live permission table and the audit filter reads `GET /api/admin/audit/actions` (a BFF test fails when the list and the actions the code writes drift); `lib/domain.ts`'s `StepStatus` is `PipelineStepStatus`; artifact versions are read only from MLMR's documented `model-artifact:<id>:<n>` form; a failed sign-out, a refused clipboard copy and unmounted toasts are handled; `useHashTab` subscribes once.

### PR-GUI-9 meets PR-SEC-10.9 / PR-MGT-2.6 — what the merge with `main` decided

The console redesign and `main`'s scope and MSAC work (#400) changed the same RAN NF OAM reads. Merged by keeping both rules on every route:
a list is narrowed by the caller's scope claim, then by its MSAC read rules, then by the console's place filters (`region`, `site_cluster`).

- **The console's aggregates follow the lists.** `GET /alarms/counts`, `/alarms/stats`, `/topology/links/counts`, `/managed-entities/health`,
  `/worst` and `/scopes` did not exist when MGT-2.6 filtered the lists, so they counted elements a managed caller may not read. Each now applies
  `msac.readable` (the link counts the same `restrict` as `GET /topology/links`, `_link_world`), and `GET /alarms/{id}/correlated` is a 404 for an
  alarm on an unreadable element. `tests/test_msac_lists.py` puts the two element lists in its route table and checks each count against what the
  caller's list shows; `tests/test_scope_reads.py` classifies the seven routes as about elements and sweeps them with a claim that matches nothing.
- **The decision-record export is internal-only at R1.** `GET /decision-records/export.csv` (GUI-9.5) holds the rows of `GET /decision-records`,
  which is internal-only because it names other rApps' actions; the export was reachable by an rApp. `smo_shared/roles.py` now covers both, and
  r1-termination's `test_roles.py` names it.
- **Revisions renumbered.** `main` took `0034` and `0035` (two-person approval, lifecycle notices); the console's revisions follow as `0036`
  (alarm ack time, site cluster), `0037` (training progress, intent fulfilment, DME delivery) and `0038` (FOCOM utilisation index).
- **The two-person approval and the onboarding / campaign screens** (#401, #402) were written against the old page files. The approval is carried
  into the Approvals (a `Votes` section, a first-or-last-approval decision box), Decisions and Safeguards folders. The onboarding templates and the
  Apply / Select actions join the redesign's existing `/configuration#onboarding` box, and the campaign job timeout, rollback order and "timed out"
  badge join `/software`, rather than reviving main's two Infrastructure tabs; the failure notices (`LifecycleWatchers`) show on both. Main's tests
  moved with them. `lib/lifecycle.ts`'s campaign helpers stay tested but unused: the Software page keeps its own (`data/form.ts`), so a change to
  the campaign rules is made in both until one is removed.

### PR-GUI-1, 2 and 6 steps closed by the redesign; one set of campaign rules; SEC-5.5's row narrowed

Bookkeeping after PR-GUI-9 (#430), checked against the code before each step was closed.

- **GUI-1.1 to 1.4, live updates.** `GET /api/events` is the Server-Sent Events endpoint on the BFF; one poller per process recomputes the
  subscribed summary pages server-side and sends only what changed (1.1). `src/data/events.ts` and `shell/LiveEvents.tsx` are the client, with
  reconnect and back-off, tested with a fake `EventSource` (1.2). The alarm console shows "N new alarms — show" and refetches its list when an
  alarm count changes (1.3). Instance and deployment state: the `rapps` and `infrastructure` summaries count instances and NF deployments per
  state, and a changed count refetches `/rapp-mgmt/instances` or `/nfo/deployments` (`LIST_PATHS`), so a state change appears without a reload
  (1.4). GUI-1.5 (the event bus as the source) stays open.
- **GUI-2.1 and 2.2, the alarm console.** The list filters on the server (severity, ack state, open only, probable cause, time, region, site
  cluster; keyset paging), and Acknowledge / Clear are `rbac.py` rules (`PATCH /ran-nf-oam/alarms/{id}/ack` and `/clear`, operator). GUI-2.3 and
  2.4 wait on MGT-8; GUI-2.5 (an alarm CSV export) does not depend on MGT-8 any more and stays open.
- **MGT-8.4, the alarm list filters.** `GET /ran-nf-oam/alarms` filters by `severity`, `ack_state`, `open_only` (the state), `since` / `until`
  (raisedAt; the time range) and `managed_element_ref`, in SQL, built for the alarm console (PR-GUI-9b, 9.4); route tests in
  `ran-nf-oam/tests/test_console_reads.py` and `test_main.py`. The rest of PR-MGT-8 (history, comments, repeats, aging, suppression) stays open.
- **GUI-6.1 and 6.2, accessibility.** `scripts/gui_e2e.py` runs axe (WCAG 2 AA) over every page, and over four pages in both themes with every
  accent, in CI ("Every page of the GUI opens, and passes the accessibility checks"); the findings were fixed to zero serious or critical. i18n
  (6.3, 6.4) stays open.
- **Not closed.** GUI-3 (the Topology page draws neighbour relations from cell guards, not the containment tree, and has no alarm overlay; a note
  says so in `OPEN_ITEMS.md`), GUI-4 (no KPI series over time; the KPI route takes no region, so the scope picker does not reach it), GUI-5 (the
  scope picker narrows what is shown; it is not a scope claim in the session), GUI-7.1 and 7.3.
- **One set of campaign rules.** #402 brought `lib/lifecycle.ts`'s campaign helpers while the redesign's Software page had its own
  (`pages/software/data/form.ts`, `data/types.ts`), so a rule change had to be made twice. The unused copy is gone (`CAMPAIGN_MEANING`,
  `HALT_MEANING`, `describeHalt`, the campaign form and payload, `campaignActions`, `waveProgress`, `describeSelector`, `describeSettings`);
  `lib/lifecycle.ts` keeps the onboarding rules. The Software page's form gained the backend's two length limits and the 5000-element cap it
  lacked (`CampaignRequest` in `ran-nf-oam/app/lifecycle.py`); the former copy's wave-size, pause and alarm caps were not carried over, because
  the backend has none and they would have refused requests it accepts. Its two test tables (every campaign state, every invalid form) now run
  against the kept functions.
- **SEC-5.5.** The cache default was decided in #399 (30 s, PR-SEC-5.5) but its row still read as the owner's open decision; the row now holds
  only what is still open there, a revocation broadcast between gateway replicas.

### PR-SEC-15 (part 2) — ownership and identity checks on routes that lacked them (SEC-15.1, 15.4, 15.7, 15.8, 15.9, 15.10 in part)

Found by the security review of October 2026 (registered as `OPEN_ITEMS.md` `PR-SEC-15` by the shared-library hardening change; the rows below are the ones this change closes, so they leave that table). Six routes took the caller's word, or no one's, for something the platform knows. Each gets a test that fails without the fix.

- **SEC-15.1, `POST /aimgf/models/{id}/advance` was open to an rApp token.** The admin check lived only in the GUI BFF's rule table; the gateway's allow-list (`roles.RAPP_MAY_CHANGE`) names `advance` because the SDK calls it. Now the route refuses a caller whose `X-R1-Role` is `rapp` with 403 `ROLE_NOT_PERMITTED`, before the event is even parsed; role `internal` (the console, an SMO module) and a call with no role (a test, an in-process call) pass, as they do in the other modules. **Not taken:** removing the route from `RAPP_MAY_CHANGE`, which would make the gateway refuse it too. That file is changed by the shared-library hardening change in flight, and `r1-termination/tests/test_roles.py` ties the list to the SDK's calls, so the removal (and the SDK method's retirement for rApps) is a follow-up once that change merges. **Not taken:** an admin-versus-operator split at the route: the module has no notion of the console's two tiers (the BFF has), and the decision "an rApp must not decide for itself" is what the finding asked for. `decided_by` of `advance` is still a query value the caller sends; the console's rules do not force it (it is the same class as SEC-15.8, not in this change).
- **SEC-15.4, the job actions and the software update.** `kpi-check`, `continue`, `halt` and `abort` of a configuration job had none of the checks the rollback has. They now go through one helper, `_job_in_reach` (the rollback uses it too): 404 `CONFIG_JOB_NOT_FOUND` for another scoped rApp's job (`PR-SEC-10.11`), then 403 `SCOPE_DENIED`, recorded as a safeguard refusal, for a job that wrote outside the caller's scope (`PR-SEC-10.4`), then the job's state (409). The ownership test is first so a caller learns nothing about a job that is not its own. `POST /software-management-jobs` is 403 `SCOPE_DENIED` for an element outside the scope (or not registered, so the answer does not say which exist); `.../{id}/advance` is a 404 for a job on one (an id the system minted). Software jobs have no owner of their own, so the rule is the scope, as for the lists. An unscoped caller is asked nothing. The gateway already refuses an rApp the routes in `test_roles.py`; the module rule matters for an SMO module acting for a scoped rApp and for a caller whose claim is set by the operator.
- **SEC-15.7, vendors.** The finding said "get and delete"; `GET /vendor-capabilities/{vendor}` was already filtered (`PR-SEC-10.9`), `DELETE` was not. It now uses the same filter: a caller with a claim deletes the entry of a vendor one of its own elements uses, and for any other entry nothing happens and the answer is 204, as for an unknown vendor and as for a subscription outside the scope. **Not taken:** a 403 for every scoped caller, because the sibling idempotent deletes answer 204 and the registry is not an element's; **a limit stated:** an entry is global, so a scoped caller whose elements use a vendor can still delete the entry that the elements of other tenants of that vendor are checked against. The gateway keeps an rApp out of the route; `PUT` is not filtered either (not in the finding). The finding named SME as the place; the routes are RAN NF OAM's.
- **SEC-15.9, rApp Management.** `POST /instances/{id}/fault` and `PUT /instances/{id}/config` had no own-instance check; a token of one instance could crash (`severity=critical`) or reconfigure another. Both now use `_own_instance_or_operator`, as `performance`, `bootstrap-complete` and `operator-api` do, before the crash is fired. The gateway does not let an rApp change these routes at all, so this is the second line.
- **SEC-15.10 (the overwrite), `POST /dme/production-capabilities`.** An existing type could be redefined by any caller. Revision `0039` (expand only) adds the nullable `dme_type.registered_by`; a new type records the invoker id the gateway vouched for (the request's `producerId` when it did not come through the gateway). A request that would change the definition of an existing type (name, schema, collection spec, source) is 403 `DME_TYPE_NOT_OWNER` unless the caller is that first registrant, has the `internal` role (the console's admin, RAN NF OAM registering its KPI types) or has no role. The same definition registered again, by the first producer or a second one, is unchanged. For a type from before the revision the producers linked to it stand in for the first one. The check precedes the producer upsert, so a refusal changes nothing, callback URLs included. **Not taken:** a timestamp on the link to find "the first" without a column (the link has none); a check that `producerId` is the caller's own id, which would break producers that register under a name of their own (RAN NF OAM does) and is **still open**: a rApp can name another producer's id and replace that producer's callback URLs. The other two parts of SEC-15.10 (`mediate_action` leaving `FORWARDED`, an unregistered `dmeTypeId`) are not in this change.
- **SEC-15.8, who decided an approval.** `decidedBy` came from the request body, and in the console's case the BFF wrote it there. Precondition checked first, as asked: the BFF did **not** forward the person to the gateway. Every call used the BFF's one SMO token, so a module saw `internal` and the BFF's invoker id; the human was only in the body fields the BFF forced (`requestedBy`, `decidedBy`). The change is small, so it was made. (1) `smo_shared/invoker.py` gains `ACTING_USER_HEADER` (`X-R1-Acting-User`) and `acting_user(request)`, which believes the header only when the role is `internal`. It is a header of its own, not `X-R1-On-Behalf-Of`, because that one names an rApp and the kill switch, rate limit, ownership and scope key on it. (2) R1 Termination drops an inbound value (it had been forwarded verbatim, like any header it does not know) and forwards the console's own, from an `internal` caller only. (3) The BFF sets `smo-gui:<username>` on every call it proxies, after filtering the browser's headers. (4) RAN NF OAM's approve and reject take the decider from it: an `internal` call without it is 403 `APPROVER_IDENTITY_REQUIRED`; a body `decidedBy` that is not that person (compared as `_same_person` does, case and space ignored) is 403 `APPROVER_IDENTITY_MISMATCH`; one that is the same person is accepted, and the stored name is the header's. `decidedBy` is optional and marked `deprecated` in the OpenAPI document; it is dropped next minor. A call with no role at all is taken at its word, as the rest of the module takes such a call, and then needs `decidedBy` (422). The self-decision rule, the two-person count and the decision record all read the verified name, so one person cannot count twice by sending two names (403 for the second name, 409 for the same user). **Upgrade order:** the console and the gateway first or together; an old console sends no header and its approvals are refused until it is upgraded (stated in `CHANGELOG.md`). **Not taken:** falling back to the body when an `internal` call has no acting user, which would keep the hole open for any SMO module; and reading the header in any module but RAN NF OAM (the other places that take a person from the body, for example AIMgF's `decided_by`, are not changed).
- **Verified here.** `ruff`; `scripts/run_mypy.sh` for the six touched modules; `scripts/check_code_docs.py`; `scripts/check_breaking_changes.py` against `smo-v0.7.0` (0 breaks: `decidedBy` became optional and deprecated, which is not one); every module suite, `gui-bff`, `shared`, `sdk` and the four samples; `tests_integration` except the Schemathesis contract lane (`test_contract_schemathesis.py`, hours long here: stopped at 20 % with no failure; CI's), the load, stress and soak files and the ones that need Postgres, Docker or helm (skipped). Revision `0039` was **not** run against Postgres here (none available); it is one `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`, and `test_migrations.py` and `check_migration_matches_models.py` are CI's. The new tests were each run against the code without the fix and fail there.
